Check the visa type and job title yourself before paying (see [Step 7: Check the visa yourself](#step-7-check-the-visa-yourself)).
- **Switched contract:** the contract handed over at the airport, or the one on arrival, can name a different employer, job or wage <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>. Compare it with the attested contract (see [Step 2: See the attested demand letter](#step-2-see-the-attested-demand-letter)) and, after arrival, with your Qiwa contract, which is the one that counts ([After you arrive](#after-you-arrive)) <sup>[21](#o21), [N237](#n237)</sup>; being sent to a different job is grounds for a BMET complaint (see ["If an agency, broker or relative takes your money"](#if-an-agency-broker-or-relative-takes-your-money)) <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Bribes for a PKB loan:** see [If you need to borrow](#if-you-need-to-borrow).
- **Bought "fit" medical reports:** see [Don't buy a "fit" result](#dont-buy-a-fit-result).
- **Fixing the skill test for money:** see [Step 3, item 4: Pay USD 85](#step-3-pass-the-skill-test-item-4).
- **Paid booking sites or brokers for the Tasheer slip:** see [Step 5, item 1: Book free](#step-5-give-fingerprints-at-the-tasheer-visa-centre-item-1).
- **Forged police clearance certificates:** see [Step 6: Give the agency your papers for the visa (start before Step 5)](#step-6-give-the-agency-your-papers-for-the-visa-start-before-step-5).
- **"Embassy fees" or "visa fees":** see [Pay no "embassy fee" or "visa fee" to anyone](#pay-no-embassy-fee-or-visa-fee-to-anyone).
- **Paying to pass a course or clearance:** see Do the compulsory 3-day pre-departure orientation (PDO).
- **Huroob removal for a fee:** see "If your employer reports you absent (huroob)" <sup>[N202](#n202)</sup>.
- **Requests for your Absher or Qiwa codes,** which let someone act in your name <sup>[N127 (2024)](#n127)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Are SIMs opened under a worker's iqama without their knowledge a known scam, and how does a worker check which phone numbers are registered under their iqama and cancel ones they didn't open?
  > - Which scams do workers themselves try on this route, and what happens to those caught?

In July 2024 the foreign minister, Hasan Mahmud, told Bangladeshis at the Riyadh embassy that a Bangladesh-Saudi joint task force would work to protect people wanting Saudi jobs from fraud, and the prime minister's adviser Salman F Rahman said they should verify that the employer is genuine before leaving (how: see [Before you pay anyone](#before-you-pay-anyone)) <sup>[N238 (2024)](#n238)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the Bangladesh-Saudi joint task force announced in July 2024 exist, and can a worker report a fraud to it?

**Cases** (allegations in case filings, not verdicts)

- 15 May 2024: in a case filed on 27 October 2024 at the Barishal Human Trafficking Offence Suppression Tribunal, a father alleged that his son Md. Rubel, on an offer from Abu Taher Sheikh, paid Tk 3.8 lakh to Md. Rana, an official of MR International in Chairmanbari, Dhaka, and Rana's sister Habiba Akter, was sent to Saudi Arabia on 15 May 2024, given no job and handed to a Saudi trafficking gang that tortured him and demanded Tk 5 lakh from his father on threat of killing him <sup>[N239 (2024)](#n239)</sup>.
- October 2024 (SJ Group): in a case filed on 28 October 2024 at the same tribunal, a father from Mehendiganj alleged that Araf Hossain alias Sifat, managing director of the recruiting agency SJ Group in Purana Paltan, Dhaka, and two local men took Tk 4.5 lakh each, Tk 1 crore 3 lakh 50 thousand in all, from 23 men from Barishal on the promise of SR 1,800 a month at a solar panel firm, Taj Al Nawar Trading East; according to the case, after they were taken to Saudi Arabia on 4 May the men were put on a Hajj project for a month and a half and paid SR 615, then 12 were caught by Saudi police as illegal migrants and sent home and 11 could not be traced <sup>[N239 (2024)](#n239)</sup>. What the tribunal did with both cases: see ["If an agency, broker or relative takes your money"](#if-an-agency-broker-or-relative-takes-your-money).

<a id="avoid-a-free-visa"></a>

#### 2.10.1 Avoid a "free visa"

A "free" (azad) visa is a real visa bought from a sponsor who has no job for you: you must find your own work and housing, work illegally, often pay the sponsor a monthly cut and renewal money (see ["What you pay, and what you earn and keep"](#what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep), On a free visa you pay it yourself), and can be deported <sup>[N8](#n8), [N54 (2020)](#n54), [N43 (2024)](#n43)</sup>. BMET warns that no visa is free <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>.

**Signs of a free visa:**

- a household sponsor, not a company (see [Who the sponsor is](#who-the-sponsor-is)) <sup>[N54 (2020)](#n54)</sup>;
- an amel adi (ordinary labourer) or load-unload occupation <sup>[N191](#n191)</sup>;
- a 3-month iqama <sup>[N207 (2022)](#n207), [N43 (2024)](#n43)</sup>;
- a contract in Saudi Arabia that isn't the one signed in Bangladesh <sup>[N54 (2020)](#n54)</sup>.

**What happens on arrival.** OKUP (Ovibashi Karmi Unnayan Program, a migrant workers' organisation) surveyed 1,084 households of workers who went to Kuwait, Oman, Qatar, the UAE and Saudi Arabia in 2022 (released October 2025): 43% got no work and 57% got a work permit; 21% found no work and paid about Tk 1.5 lakh more for an iqama, 4% paid Tk 44,000 to get work, 4% came home at their own cost, and 14% could not pay for a permit or the trip home and stayed on undocumented <sup>[N44](#n44)</sup>. A case: Md. Habib Khan (21) of Nabakalas, Matlab Dakshin, Chandpur, borrowed Tk 5.5 lakh and went in November 2023 on a free visa through a Narayanganj broker, Md. Rashed; after working a while at a residential hotel in Mecca he left, then under a local broker got only occasional small jobs with little pay and spent most of his time idle, according to his family and local sources <sup>[N240](#n240)</sup>.

Have the papers checked (see [Before you pay anyone](#before-you-pay-anyone)) <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>; report the seller to BMET or the ministry (see ["If an agency, broker or relative takes your money"](#if-an-agency-broker-or-relative-takes-your-money)) or on the ministry hotline 16135 (+880 9610102030 from abroad) <sup>[1](#o1), [69](#o69), [21](#o21)</sup>; in an emergency call 999, the police emergency number <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.

<a id="after-you-arrive"></a>

### 2.11 After you arrive

<a id="residence-card-iqama-and-medical"></a>

#### 2.11.1 Residence card (iqama) and medical

You enter on a 3-month permit; within it the employer must get your iqama and work permit, and it pays for them and their renewals (what that costs the employer: see [The iqama: the employer's bill](#the-iqama-the-employers-bill); a 2024 report put it at about SR 11,000 a year) <sup>[23](#o23), [35](#o35), [N152 (2024)](#n152)</sup>. The iqama is issued after your medical test and once the employer has insured you <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>; you do the medical test and fingerprints, and it takes 1-3 weeks once papers are complete <sup>[N241](#n241)</sup>. Without an iqama you become undocumented and can be detained and deported <sup>[N152 (2024)](#n152)</sup>. Check the name matches your passport; the employer corrects it at the Passports Directorate (Jawazat) <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>. Carry it always and show it to police on request <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>. Without it you can't open a bank account, use an ATM, send money or get a SIM <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.

*The renewal medical:* in Saudi Arabia and other Gulf countries, HIV is usually found at the compulsory medical check for work-permit renewal or when a sick worker goes to hospital, after which the visa is cancelled and the worker is quickly sent home; in one case a Bangladeshi construction worker in an unnamed Middle Eastern country was sent home within a few days of testing positive, with no chance to discuss treatment or counselling <sup>[N242](#n242)</sup>.

*No iqama after 90 days:* complain to the labour ministry on 19911 (Sunday to Thursday, 8 am to 5 pm) <sup>[23](#o23), [78](#o78)</sup>; then take the steps for unpaid wages (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)) and call the embassy labour wing on 8001000125 <sup>[79](#o79)</sup>.

<a id="set-up-absher-and-check-your-qiwa-contract"></a>

#### 2.11.2 Set up Absher and check your Qiwa contract

Do both in your first week <sup>[N237](#n237)</sup>.

**Set up Absher,** the Interior Ministry's portal for your iqama, visas and appointments <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.

**You'll need**
<sup>[N130](#n130)</sup>:

- your iqama number;
- a Saudi mobile number.

1. On [absher.sa](https://www.absher.sa/) press New User, enter your iqama number, a password and the SMS code <sup>[N130](#n130)</sup>.
2. Activate it with Nafath, the Saudi digital ID app <sup>[N130](#n130)</sup>, or at an Absher kiosk in a mall or Jawazat office (iqama number, both index fingers, mobile number, code) <sup>[N130](#n130)</sup>.
3. If that fails, book an appointment on Absher and activate it free at a Jawazat office <sup>[N130](#n130)</sup>.

Support: 920020405 <sup>[N130](#n130)</sup>.

**Check your contract on Qiwa,** the labour ministry's platform, where every contract must be recorded <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.

**You'll need**
<sup>[N237](#n237)</sup>:

- your iqama number;
- your date of birth as on the iqama;
- Nafath.

1. Register at [sso.qiwa.sa](https://sso.qiwa.sa/en/registration) and confirm your identity with Nafath <sup>[N237](#n237)</sup>.
2. The employer sends the contract to your account. What you approve there is the record of your pay and terms, so before approving check that the job title and wage match your attested contract ([Step 2: See the attested demand letter](#step-2-see-the-attested-demand-letter)) and what you were promised, understand every term, and keep a copy <sup>[N237](#n237), [81](#o81), [9](#o9)</sup>.

Support: 920000105, support@qiwa.sa <sup>[N237](#n237)</sup>.

<a id="welfare-board-sign-up-if-you-left-without-bmet-clearance"></a>

#### 2.11.3 Welfare Board sign-up if you left without BMET clearance

**If you came without BMET clearance,** join the Welfare Board from Saudi Arabia to get its insurance and help: apply at [portal.wewb.gov.bd](https://portal.wewb.gov.bd/) with your passport and proof you work legally; the embassy labour wing processes it and you pay the riyal equivalent of Tk 6,500, life insurance included <sup>[82](#o82)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How much in riyals does the labour wing now charge for Welfare Board membership?

<a id="your-rights-at-work"></a>

### 2.12 Your rights at work

If a right is broken: see [Where to complain](#where-to-complain).

<a id="contract"></a>

#### 2.12.1 Contract

Your contract must be recorded on Qiwa ([After you arrive](#after-you-arrive)), with a copy for you and the employer <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. It runs for a fixed term, usually 2 years; with no length written it counts as one year, and it renews for the same length if both sides carry on <sup>[26](#o26), [9](#o9)</sup>. It should state your leave, whether it is paid and who pays the airfare <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.

**Probation**
Probation can last up to 180 days if the contract sets it; either side can end the contract during it without compensation or end-of-service pay <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. Who pays the ticket home: see [If you resign or are dismissed](#if-you-resign-or-are-dismissed).

<a id="pay-and-deductions"></a>

#### 2.12.2 Pay and deductions

**How you are paid**
There is no minimum wage for foreign workers; the contract wage is what you can claim <sup>[N127 (2024)](#n127)</sup>. The employer must pay it on time, into your bank account on the contract date, through the Wage Protection Program, which records each payment <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. Never sign that you were paid before the money arrives; it can block a later claim <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How can a worker see their recorded wage payments?

**What can't be taken from your pay**
Recruitment, iqama and work permit fees and renewals, job-title changes, exit and re-entry visas, transfer fees and your ticket home at the contract's end are the employer's and can't be taken from your pay <sup>[9](#o9), [35](#o35)</sup>. Some brokers, small kafils (sponsors) and labour-supply companies still cut the iqama and health-insurance cost from wages <sup>[N129](#n129)</sup>. If the health-insurance premium is taken: see Housing, food and health care.

**What can be taken**
These rules come from the Labour Law (Royal Decree M/51 of 27 September 2005), in the English text on the labour ministry's website <sup>[50 (2023)](#o50)</sup>. Amendments to the law took effect on 19 February 2025 <sup>[N144 (2024)](#n144)</sup>. Without your written consent the employer may deduct only what the law lists, such as repayment of its loan (at most 10% of the wage), fines for your violations and damage you cause <sup>[50 (2023)](#o50)</sup>. A fine for one violation is at most five days' wage, and fines taken in one month are at most five days' wage <sup>[50 (2023)](#o50)</sup>. For loss or damage you cause, by your own fault, to the employer's machinery or products, the employer may take what repair costs but at most five days' wage a month <sup>[50 (2023)](#o50)</sup>. You can dispute the claim or the amount at the labour court within 15 work days of the employer telling you; if the court finds for you, the employer must return what it took wrongly within seven days <sup>[50 (2023)](#o50)</sup>. All deductions together may not exceed half your wage, unless the labour court allows more <sup>[50 (2023)](#o50)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Did the February 2025 amendments change the deduction and fine limits (Articles 70, 91, 92 and 93)?

**Market pay**
What a new Bangladeshi recruit is really paid: see [What the job pays](#what-the-job-pays). General market pay in Saudi Arabia <sup>[N243](#n243)</sup>:

- helpers: SR 1,200-1,800;
- cleaners: SR 1,000-1,700;
- construction workers and masons: SR 1,600-3,000;
- drivers: SR 1,500-2,500;
- electricians: SR 1,800-3,500.

<a id="hours-rest-and-leave"></a>

#### 2.12.3 Hours, rest and leave

You work at most 8 hours a day and 48 a week (6 and 36 for Muslims in Ramadan), never over 12 a day in total <sup>[9](#o9), [26](#o26)</sup>, with a break of at least 30 minutes after 5 hours in a row <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. From 15 June to 15 September, no private-sector work in direct sun from 12 noon to 3 pm; report a breach on 19911 or in the labour ministry's app (see [Where to complain](#where-to-complain)) <sup>[83](#o83), [84](#o84), [85](#o85)</sup>. Heat stroke (confusion, slurred speech, fainting, seizures, very high temperature, hot dry or drenched skin) kills if treatment is delayed: get emergency care, move the person into shade, take off outer clothing and cool them with water <sup>[N244](#n244)</sup>. Headache, nausea, dizziness, weakness and heavy sweating are heat exhaustion: get out of the heat, drink and get checked at a clinic <sup>[N244](#n244)</sup>. Migration research bodies said in October 2026 that many Bangladeshis in the Middle East work in construction in extreme daytime heat, which can damage the kidneys, brain and other organs, worsen breathing, heart, diabetes and kidney disease, and cause premature death <sup>[N245](#n245)</sup>.

**Overtime**
Overtime pays your full hourly wage plus half the basic hourly wage per extra hour; public-holiday work counts as overtime <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.

**Rest and leave**
- *Weekly rest:* one day off a week, normally Friday, not swappable for money <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.
- *Annual leave:* paid, at least 21 days, 30 after 5 years with the same employer, on dates the employer sets with 30 days' notice; untaken days are paid when you leave <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.
- *Paid holidays:* 4 days at each Eid, Founding Day (22 February), National Day (23 September) <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.
- *Sick leave:* up to 120 days a year, 30 at full pay, 60 at three-quarters, 30 unpaid <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.
- *Other paid leave:* 3 days for a child's birth, 5 for your wedding, 5 for the death of a spouse, parent or child, 10 to 15 days once for Hajj after 2 years; 12 weeks' maternity leave <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.

<a id="housing-food-and-health-care"></a>

#### 2.12.4 Housing, food and health care

The employer gives housing and transport or allowances for them <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>; food only if the contract says so <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. In August 2025 the adviser, Asif Nazrul, said he had seen migrant workers at one place in Saudi Arabia living in conditions worse than anyone lives in Bangladesh's worst slum <sup>[N102](#n102)</sup>. The workers killed in the 9 August 2026 fire at a Riyadh sofa factory were asleep in the factory, and its main gate was locked from outside so they could not get out, according to the uncle of one of them <sup>[N122](#n122)</sup>.

**Health insurance**
The employer must insure you, but the cover doesn't pay for serious illness, so go only if you are fit <sup>[9](#o9), [23](#o23)</sup>. Check your cover with the Council of Health Insurance's Insurance Information Inquiry at [chi.gov.sa](https://www.chi.gov.sa/en/ServicesDirectory/Pages/Eservices-CheckInsurance.aspx) <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which number does the check ask for (iqama or passport)?
> - What does the result show?

If you aren't insured or the premium is taken from your pay, complain at [care.chi.gov.sa](https://care.chi.gov.sa/) (guest or account) with your details, the employer's contact details, what happened and scanned proof; it is handled within 15 working days without naming you to the employer <sup>[87 (2024)](#o87)</sup>.

<a id="your-passport-1"></a>

#### 2.12.5 Your passport

No employer may keep your passport or iqama, not even "for safekeeping"; the fine is SR 5,000, though sponsors still commonly do it <sup>[9](#o9), [N127 (2024)](#n127), [23](#o23)</sup>. To complain, call 19911 <sup>[78](#o78)</sup> or use the labour ministry's Beneficiary Page ([selfservice.hrsd.gov.sa](https://selfservice.hrsd.gov.sa/node/)) <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>: [hrsd.gov.sa](https://www.hrsd.gov.sa/en/ministry-services/services/client-page) > Contact Us > Beneficiary Page > Start the Service > log in with Nafath > Complaints > Add a Request; choose the sector and categories, fill in the fields, press Send <sup>[88 (2024)](#o88)</sup>. The page needs a ministry or Absher account, is free, answered within 5 days, and also in the ministry's app <sup>[89](#o89)</sup>.

<a id="staying-long-term"></a>

### 2.13 Staying long-term

<a id="how-long-you-can-stay"></a>

#### 2.13.1 How long you can stay

You can stay as long as you have a contract and a valid iqama, which the employer renews and pays for <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. Work visas are issued for ages 18 to 60 <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.

<a id="pension"></a>

#### 2.13.2 Pension

There is no Saudi pension for foreign workers: GOSI, the Saudi social insurance agency, covers them only for work injuries <sup>[90](#o90)</sup>; you get end-of-service pay instead ([Leaving at the end of the job](#leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job)) <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. On the Bangladesh side, the foreign minister told Bangladeshis in Madinah in June 2024 that the government had launched a special pension scheme for expatriates whose benefit even people aged 55 can take, and that offshore banking lets expatriates open dollar accounts easily <sup>[N246 (2024)](#n246)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the expatriates' pension scheme called, what does it cost, and how does a worker in Saudi Arabia join it?

<a id="renewing-your-passport-and-getting-an-nid"></a>

#### 2.13.3 Renewing your passport and getting an NID

**Passport.** To renew it at a Bangladesh mission:

1. Book "E-Passport Service" on [passportriyadh.com/appointment](https://passportriyadh.com/appointment/), at the embassy in Riyadh or another city, with your name, phone and passport number <sup>[91](#o91)</sup>.
2. Fill in the form online <sup>[92](#o92)</sup>.
3. Bring your old passport and your NID or an online birth registration certificate in English <sup>[92](#o92)</sup>.

A worker's 48-page, 5-year e-passport costs SR 125 (SR 190 urgent), a 10-year one SR 210 <sup>[N247 (2024)](#n247)</sup>.

Renew before it runs low: an exit and re-entry visa needs your passport valid 90 days, a final exit 60 days <sup>[29](#o29)</sup>. At a Jeddah consulate seminar in February 2025, community leaders demanded that no migrant worker be harassed when renewing a passport at the missions, and that the government budget pay to send home the bodies of migrants who die abroad <sup>[N90](#n90)</sup>.

**Rohingya on Bangladeshi passports.** In February 2025 the Saudi ambassador in Dhaka said Saudi Arabia had asked Bangladesh to renew the Bangladeshi passports of 69,000 Rohingya living in Saudi Arabia, only for legal processes, and would not send them back to Bangladesh <sup>[N248](#n248)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long does it take?
> - How do you pay?
> - What is the Jeddah consulate's booking route?

**NID.** If you are over 16 and never had one, fill in the form at [services.nidw.gov.bd](https://services.nidw.gov.bd/nid-pub/), then on the appointment date hand in the documents at the mission and give a photo, fingerprints, iris scan and signature <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>; missions in Saudi Arabia had begun NID registration by July 2025 <sup>[N249](#n249)</sup>. A mission can't correct an existing NID <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How do you book NID registration at the Riyadh embassy or Jeddah consulate?
> - How does a worker abroad correct an NID that doesn't match the passport?

In July 2024 Bangladeshis in Riyadh asked the foreign minister for easier iqama renewal, a direct Chattogram-Riyadh flight, health insurance, a proper system for bringing bodies home, legal aid, passport renewal through the embassy, correction of NID details and family visa renewal; he promised to address them <sup>[N238 (2024)](#n238)</sup>.

**Voting.** According to an October 2025 column, the Election Commission planned to pilot voting by Bangladeshis abroad in Saudi Arabia, Malaysia, the UAE and the UK, first at embassy booths, with voters registering online in advance and voting on the set day after passport and NID checks <sup>[N80](#n80)</sup>. For the 12 February 2026 national election, the first in which Bangladeshis abroad could vote, by postal ballot, more than 239,000 Bangladeshis in Saudi Arabia registered, according to Election Commission figures <sup>[N250](#n250)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker in Saudi Arabia register for a postal ballot for the next election?

<a id="finishing-your-studies"></a>

#### 2.13.4 Finishing your studies

According to the Bangladesh embassy in Riyadh in January 2024, workers in Saudi Arabia who could not finish their studies can sit SSC, HSC and degree examinations through the Open University, on the embassy's initiative <sup>[N57 (2024)](#n57)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker register with the embassy for Open University exams, and where are they held?

<a id="going-home-on-leave-and-coming-back"></a>

#### 2.13.5 Going home on leave and coming back

Apply for an exit and re-entry visa yourself on Absher; the employer is notified, its approval isn't needed, and it pays the fee <sup>[28](#o28), [9](#o9)</sup>:

- SR 200 for one trip up to 2 months;
- SR 100 per extra month;
- SR 500 for multiple trips over 3 months <sup>[32 (2024)](#o32), [N251](#n251)</sup>.

You must be in Saudi Arabia, with <sup>[29](#o29), [N140](#n140)</sup>:

- a passport valid at least 90 days;
- fingerprints registered;
- no unpaid traffic or other fines;
- a valid iqama and Qiwa contract;
- for a trip set in months, an iqama covering 90 days plus the trip.

"Return Before" on the visa is your last day to come back <sup>[29](#o29)</sup>. Your contract says who pays the ticket <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>. At the airport in Bangladesh, the Probashi Kallyan (expatriate welfare) desk checks the re-entry visa <sup>[N252](#n252)</sup>. On Absher you declare you understand the measures taken if you don't return before your approved leave ends <sup>[N140](#n140)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What are those measures, now that the old three-year entry ban is lifted <sup>[N253 (2024)](#n253)</sup>?

**Cheaper flights home in the Hajj season.** In January 2026 the interim government set a special Biman Bangladesh Airlines fare for migrant workers of at least Tk 20,500 one way on Madinah-Dhaka and Jeddah-Dhaka and Tk 42,000 return on Madinah-Dhaka-Madinah and Jeddah-Dhaka-Jeddah, according to the Chief Adviser's press wing <sup>[N254](#n254)</sup>. It applied to travel home from 18 April to 25 May 2026 and back from 30 May to 30 June 2026, on flights that the civil aviation adviser said usually return empty during Hajj, with a target of 80,000 tickets sold in Saudi Arabia and Bangladesh <sup>[N254](#n254)</sup>. On 18 June 2026 the civil aviation minister, Afroza Khanam, told parliament the fare was Tk 20,000, against Tk 50,000-55,000 before on the same routes <sup>[N234](#n234)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Will the worker fare run again in the 2027 Hajj season, and is it Tk 20,000 or Tk 20,500 one way?

**When the trip back goes wrong.** Md Shahed Ahmmad of Bogura, a plumber with an iqama on a Saudi contractor's Jeddah project, was returning to work through Riyadh after leave in Bangladesh when he was one of two Bangladeshis killed in the 10 October 2026 attack on Riyadh airport <sup>[N25](#n25)</sup>. Abul Hossain of Senbag, Noakhali, a tile mason six years in Saudi Arabia, had booked his return three months ahead, but his 12 October 2026 Biman flight to Dammam was cancelled, and he said that if he could not go back he would struggle to keep his job <sup>[N31](#n31)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - If cancelled flights keep a worker in Bangladesh past "Return Before", can the employer extend the re-entry visa, and does the worker lose the job?

<a id="bringing-your-family"></a>

#### 2.13.6 Bringing your family

Most new Bangladeshi labourers can't bring family to live: family residence is open only to a worker whose permit is graded skilled, and elementary jobs such as cleaners and labourers, the jobs most new Bangladeshi labourers do, are graded basic (the full rules, including the salary needed: see [Family residence](#family-residence)) <sup>[94](#o94), [81](#o81), [N9](#n9)</sup>. Any worker's family can visit for up to 90 days (conditions: see [Family visit visa](#family-visit-visa)) <sup>[95](#o95)</sup>. Each work permit is graded High-skilled, Skilled or Basic on the employer's Qiwa account; you can ask for a wrong grade to be corrected, which depends on the job title in your Qiwa contract being right <sup>[81](#o81)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a worker see their own grade?
> - Does High-skilled count as skilled here?

<a id="what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported"></a>

### 2.14 What can get you jailed or deported

- **Don't bring** narcotic drugs, alcohol, anything with pork, poppy seeds (khas khas), khat, gutkha, pan masala, non-Islamic religious literature, obscene material, amulets or anything linked to black magic, including on your phone <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.
- **Never carry a parcel you haven't packed and checked yourself,** even for family <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>; Saudi Arabia executes foreigners for drug smuggling <sup>[N255](#n255)</sup>. Being forced to carry drugs doesn't help: in two drug cases Amnesty International documented (July 2025), the accused said they had been forced to carry them, but the court neither examined nor accepted this <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>.
  - *The scale:* Amnesty counted 597 executions for drug offences alone from January 2014 to June 2025, about 75% of them foreigners <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>, and at least 356 executions of all kinds in 2025, foreigners included <sup>[N83](#n83)</sup>. The European Saudi Organisation for Human Rights (ESOHR), a Berlin-based rights group, recorded 108 executions in January-June 2026, 59 of them foreigners; 71 were for drug offences, 49 of those foreigners, and 84 of the 108 were discretionary (taazir) sentences <sup>[N256](#n256), [N86](#n86)</sup>. Its list includes Bangladeshis, but not how many, and does not include the two Bangladeshis executed in the Makkah mosque case <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - How many Bangladeshis were executed in Saudi Arabia in 2026, and for what offences?

- **Medicines:** your own only, at most 30 days' supply, in original packs, with a medical report and a prescription under 6 months old, both translated into Arabic <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.
- **Don't work for anyone but the employer on your permit, or in another job,** even for a few hours <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. Arifur Rahman, a Bangladeshi doctor in Saudi Arabia, said police took and questioned him as a suspect on a complaint that he, a doctor, was involved in recruiting workers, a breach of his iqama rules he did not know of; he was released after about three months when it was not proved <sup>[N103](#n103)</sup>.
- **Don't join a protest or public gathering:** gatherings of any kind are banned in the Middle East; some Bangladeshis were reported arrested in Saudi Arabia in 2024 for protesting in support of the July 2024 student movement, and a UAE court sentenced 57 Bangladeshis for street protests, 53 to 10 years, one to 11 years and three to life <sup>[N257 (2024)](#n257)</sup>.
- **Don't help anyone do Hajj without a permit:** for Hajj 2025 the interior ministry said anyone who helps faces a fine of up to SR 100,000, and if it is proved a court orders the helper's own vehicle confiscated <sup>[N77](#n77)</sup>. Bangladesh's religious affairs ministry asked Bangladeshis in Saudi Arabia not to sponsor, transport, help into the restricted Hajj area, or house in hotels or homes any visit-visa holder breaking the Hajj rules, and not to perform Hajj without a permit themselves <sup>[N77](#n77)</sup>.
- **HIV or pregnancy:** Saudi Arabia does not let foreigners with HIV stay, under its immigration, public health or residence rules (how it is found: see Residence card (iqama) and medical) <sup>[N242](#n242)</sup>. Tasneem Siddiqui, acting executive director of RMMRU (Refugee and Migratory Movements Research Unit, a non-governmental organisation working on migration), said in February 2026 that in these countries a woman worker who becomes pregnant is also sent back at once <sup>[N242](#n242)</sup>.
- **An expired iqama, or none:** see Residence card (iqama) and medical.
- **Being reported absent (huroob):** see ["If your employer reports you absent (huroob)"](#if-your-employer-reports-you-absent-huroob).
- **Adultery, or an "indecent act" in a mosque,** can bring death: in October 2026 a Bangladeshi man and woman were executed for an "indecent act" inside a mosque; the man had been held under the cybercrime law and the woman on an adultery allegation <sup>[N258](#n258), [N259](#n259)</sup>. The two, Nazrul Islam Abdul Basir and Sweety Akter, were executed on 14 October 2026, three years after the 2023 incident in the Makkah mosque <sup>[N260](#n260), [N261](#n261)</sup>. The court sentenced them to death under taazir (also written tazir), the judge's discretionary punishment under Shariah law; a higher judicial authority upheld it and a royal order directed that it be carried out <sup>[N262](#n262), [N261](#n261)</sup>. The Bangladesh mission in Jeddah said there was no earlier case of a Bangladeshi sentenced to death in Saudi Arabia for a cyber offence or adultery <sup>[N263](#n263)</sup>. How their case ran: see [If you or a relative is arrested](#if-you-or-a-relative-is-arrested).
- **Cybercrime:** breaking the cybercrime law has brought Bangladeshis 6 months to 5 years in prison <sup>[N259](#n259)</sup>. Posting a video of other people counts: Sheikh Sahabuddin, an Indian cleaner at the Makkah mosque, was arrested in 2023 and jailed after posting a video of the two Bangladeshis later executed, which the authorities treated as a serious offence against the dignity of a holy place <sup>[N261](#n261)</sup>. So does war content: in early March 2026 the Bangladesh embassy in Riyadh asked Bangladeshis not to upload photos or videos of the war, or post, share, like or comment on war news <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>, and on 12 October 2026 it said that recording, exchanging or posting any information, photo or video about the Middle East situation is a punishable offence, and asked people not to post it on Facebook, YouTube, X or other platforms, keep it on their phones or share it <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>. An academic, Mohammad Jalal Uddin Sikder of North South University, wrote in April 2026 that during the Iran war many migrant workers in the region were detained or sent home after being accused of sharing war videos or photos <sup>[N68](#n68)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Beyond war content and filming others, which online acts count as crimes under Saudi Arabia's cybercrime law (posts, messages)?

<a id="driving"></a>

#### 2.14.1 Driving

If you cause a crash that kills someone, the family can claim blood money (diya): a Riyadh court ordered an uninsured foreign driver to pay SR 300,000 per person killed and kept him in prison until he paid <sup>[N264 (2012)](#n264)</sup>. Compulsory motor insurance pays victims up to SR 10 million, but the insurer can take that money back from a driver without the right class of licence or with an expired one <sup>[96 (2020)](#o96)</sup>.

**Cases**

- 23 July 2025: Sakibur Rahman (25) of Mohuripara, Pekua, Cox's Bazar, who had arrived on 6 June 2025, died on the spot in a road accident in Mahil city while learning to drive, and a relative was injured, his elder brother said <sup>[N265](#n265)</sup>.
- December 2025: Nurul Alam (38) of Akbarpara, Adhunagar, Lohagara, Chattogram, 18 years in Saudi Arabia, who learned tailoring, worked about 10 years at a firm and then ran his own cloth business in Mahail, Abha (Asir province), delivering to shops in his own microbus, was killed on the spot in a collision with a cargo lorry on his way home after deliveries, a relative said <sup>[N266](#n266)</sup>.
- 15 February 2026: five Bangladeshis from Ramganj, Lakshmipur, died when their private car crashed in Abha on the way home after Umrah: Mizanur Rahman (42), who had long run a hotel business in Abha, his wife, two daughters and their driver Mohammad Jilani alias Babor (30); an 11-year-old daughter was seriously injured <sup>[N267](#n267)</sup>.
- 15 May 2026: Nure Alam (42) and Yunus Mia (43) of Cumilla, friends who ran a business together in Riyadh, died in a head-on collision at Afif on the Jeddah-Riyadh highway at night while bringing shop goods from Jeddah, a relative said <sup>[N268](#n268)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a worker on a work visa learn to drive and get a licence in his first months, and through which school?

<a id="if-you-or-a-relative-is-arrested"></a>

#### 2.14.2 If you or a relative is arrested

**If you are arrested.** The court provides an interpreter, but you can't choose your own <sup>[N259](#n259)</sup>. Saudi Arabia's Law of Criminal Procedure, which Prothom Alo dates to 2013, gives a defendant the right to legal assistance during investigation and trial and prohibits torture <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>. Bangladesh and Saudi Arabia are both parties to the Vienna Convention on Consular Relations, under whose Article 36 the detaining country must, at the prisoner's request, inform the prisoner's consulate without delay and allow consular assistance; it was not clear whether the two Bangladeshis executed in 2026 had asked <sup>[N262](#n262)</sup>. Don't count on the embassy knowing about your case: Saudi authorities may not tell it, even about a death sentence, even one carried out <sup>[N259](#n259)</sup>, and no lawyer was arranged for the two Bangladeshis executed in 2026 <sup>[N269](#n269)</sup>. The embassy's labour wing line is 8001000125 <sup>[79](#o79)</sup>.

**What happens in practice.** In cases of foreigners facing death sentences, ESOHR documented no professional interpreters, no legal aid, defendants not understanding the proceedings and no effective chance to defend themselves; prisoners said death-row inmates were briefly isolated and cut off before being taken suddenly for execution <sup>[N256](#n256)</sup>. ESOHR also found that death-row prisoners in Khamis Mushait, Najran and Tabuk prisons often did not know when their sentence was upheld or their pardon plea rejected <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>. Amnesty International found (July 2025) that many of 25 foreigners convicted of drug offences went through the system alone, did not understand or did not receive the court papers and charges, got no consular help, and gave confessions without adequate interpreters or lawyers <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does a court interpreter attend in practice in ordinary cases, given ESOHR found no professional interpreter in death-row cases?

**A case.** Shakil Mia (28) of Kishoreganj went to Saudi Arabia in 2019 on borrowed money through Altab Recruiting Agency of Banani, Dhaka, left his workplace because of complications with his employer over his iqama, and was arrested by Saudi police while working illegally and jailed, his uncle said <sup>[N270](#n270)</sup>. From jail he told his family he needed about Tk 2 lakh for bail to come home, and his father said they borrowed and sent about Tk 1.8 lakh to a lawyer in Saudi Arabia; Shakil died in custody on 15 May 2026 after, his family said, an air conditioner exploded and the smoke left him unable to breathe <sup>[N270](#n270)</sup>.

**If a relative or friend is arrested.** Report it to the labour wing, either on its online form, choosing the jail problem type, describing it in Bangla or English and attaching the person's iqama and passport copies <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>, or on 8001000125 <sup>[79](#o79)</sup>. Ask the mission for legal help; neither family of the two executed in 2026 had done so <sup>[N259](#n259)</sup>. In October 2026 the Jeddah consulate wrote to the Saudi ministry about two Bangladeshis whose families had reported that security forces arrested them without the families knowing why or where <sup>[N271](#n271)</sup>.

**Calls and notice.** Nazrul Islam, one of the two executed in 2026, was arrested about eight months after arriving; his family learned of it a month later, he could phone his parents for one to two minutes about once a month from prison, and on the day of execution someone from Saudi Arabia phoned the family to tell them <sup>[N176](#n176)</sup>. Saudi law has no clear rule on advance notice of an execution to the prisoner or family: families told Amnesty International that at most the prisoner can phone 24 hours before, and many learned of the execution from other prisoners' calls or from the media after the interior ministry announced it <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can an arrested worker call the embassy from custody, and does anyone provide a lawyer free?
> - What legal help does the embassy give in practice (lawyer, visits)?
> - How does a family in Bangladesh find out where a relative is held?

**What the mission knows and does.** The Jeddah consulate asks the Saudi foreign ministry for lists of Bangladeshis held in prisons in western Saudi Arabia, and the lists can leave out the sentence <sup>[N272](#n272)</sup>. The list sent on 30 September 2026 named 2,461 Bangladeshi prisoners in the consulate's area; for 22 of them, including the two later executed, the status said "sentenced" but the judgment column was blank: 8 murder, 5 drug trafficking, 5 "private right", 2 state-law violations, 1 cybercrime and 1 adultery case <sup>[N271](#n271)</sup>. It named Nazrul Islam and Sweety Akter and their offences but not their sentences, and the consul general said that had it shown the death sentences, the mission could have acted to protect them <sup>[N263](#n263)</sup>. The foreign ministry told parliament's standing committee on foreign affairs that a list of about 2,500 prisoners including their names arrived 14 days before the executions but the case was not properly identified, and that from the prisoner category officials expected 6 months to 15 years in jail <sup>[N85](#n85)</sup>. The 22 with unknown sentences were held in Jeddah's Jahban prison (6), Shumaisi (6), Jeddah General prison (4), and the prisons of Najran, Khamis, Al-Baha, Haddah and the Jeddah women's prison (6), consulate sources said <sup>[N271](#n271)</sup>. In death-penalty cases the mission usually tries legal aid, appeal and high-level diplomatic intervention, diplomatic sources said <sup>[N273](#n273)</sup>. When the government learns a Bangladeshi has been convicted abroad, the state minister for foreign affairs said in October 2026, it contacts that country's government through diplomatic channels and discusses whether the sentence can be remitted, with the Expatriates' Welfare and Home ministries <sup>[N28](#n28)</sup>; she was also quoted as saying that when something happens under Saudi law "there is not much for us to do" <sup>[N274](#n274)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What has the mission changed in how it tracks Bangladeshi prisoners since the October 2026 executions?

**Pardons, blood money and transfers.**

- *Royal pardons:* every year at Ramadan, both Eids and the Prophet's death anniversary, Middle Eastern countries let prisoners and minor offenders go home under general pardons, and the Bangladesh consulate in Saudi Arabia regularly applies for royal pardons for Bangladeshis not held for serious crimes, according to a Prothom Alo column <sup>[N275](#n275)</sup>. On 30 January 2025 Bangladesh asked Saudi Interior Vice Minister Nasser bin Abdulaziz Al Dawood for quick repatriation of irregular migrants and a royal pardon for Bangladeshis convicted of minor offences; he said he would review and consider these <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>. On 19 October 2026 the Jeddah consulate wrote to the Saudi foreign ministry grouping the 2,461 prisoners in five categories and asking for quick release and return of those whose sentences had ended, inclusion of well-behaved prisoners in the royal pardon list, remission of the rest of the sentence for first-time and minor offenders, and quick disposal of pending cases <sup>[N271](#n271)</sup>. On 26 October 2026 the state minister for expatriates' welfare, Md Nurul Haque, asked the Saudi government to include imprisoned Bangladeshis in a special pardon next Ramadan; this was a request, not a decision <sup>[N33](#n33)</sup>.
- *Murder and blood money:* under Saudi law a murder case can end in qisas (retaliation, meaning death), blood money or a prison term <sup>[N271](#n271)</sup>. In a qisas case only the victim's family can pardon: if it asks for compensation, is paid blood money and agrees to forgive, even a person sentenced to death is freed; in 2011 eight Bangladeshi workers were beheaded for killing an Egyptian guard after his family refused Bangladesh's offer of blood money <sup>[N275](#n275)</sup>.
- *Death sentences lifted:* in 2021 the governor of the Eastern Province spared a Bangladeshi from the death penalty and arranged for him to be sent home <sup>[N275](#n275)</sup>. A Sri Lankan sentenced to death in a blasphemy case had the sentence overturned by the Saudi Supreme Court after the Sri Lankan government took diplomatic and legal action, with a special legal team working alongside Saudi lawyers <sup>[N274](#n274)</sup>.
- *Serving the sentence at home:* Bangladesh has sentenced-prisoner transfer agreements only with India (2010) and the UAE (2014), none with Saudi Arabia, although in February 2024 the then home minister said Saudi Arabia was interested in a prisoner repatriation agreement <sup>[N275](#n275)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Were Bangladeshi prisoners included in a Saudi pardon in Ramadan 2027?

**The numbers.** The foreign minister told parliament on 19 February 2024 that 9,370 Bangladeshis were held in prisons in 26 countries, the most, 5,746, in Saudi Arabia, and gave the same Saudi figure on 1 July 2024, among 11,450 in 31 countries <sup>[N276 (2024)](#n276), [N132 (2024)](#n132)</sup>. On 23 October 2026 the state minister for foreign affairs, Shama Obaid Islam, said about 7,500 Bangladeshis were held in prisons in Saudi cities <sup>[N28](#n28)</sup>. ESOHR could learn nothing about 91 of the 108 cases (84.2%) before the executions of January-June 2026, and Prothom Alo found no answer on how many Bangladeshis are held, on what charges, or whether they get lawyers, interpreters or consular help <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshis are on death row in Saudi Arabia, and on what charges?

**The October 2026 executions**

- *Who they were:* according to government documents and Bangladeshi diplomats, Nazrul Islam (from Brahmanbaria) and Sweety Akter (from Tangail) went to Saudi Arabia in late 2022 and joined the Al Majal company as cleaners; Nazrul was detained on 1 August 2023 for a cybercrime or IT offence and held in the Jeddah correctional facility, and Sweety on 2 August 2023 for adultery and held in the Jeddah women's prison <sup>[N263](#n263)</sup>. Nazrul's family says he went on 10 November 2022 to work as a cleaner at the Haram in Makkah <sup>[N176](#n176)</sup>. On 19 October 2026 the expatriates' welfare minister, Ariful Haque Chowdhury, said one of the two was a migrant worker and the other an Umrah pilgrim <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>.
- *The trial:* the trial ran for several months in a Jeddah court, with two Bangladeshi citizens helping as interpreters, according to unofficial sources in Saudi Arabia <sup>[N262](#n262)</sup>. At a 19 October 2026 meeting in Jeddah the Saudi side told Bangladeshi officials that a lower court sentenced the two, an appeal-court panel of 13 judges upheld the death sentences, and they were executed in separate cells for men and women <sup>[N263](#n263)</sup>.
- *What Bangladesh knew:* on 17 October 2026 the state minister for foreign affairs said the labour wing had reported the two were engaged in "adultery" inside a Makkah mosque and that the government had received nothing formally <sup>[N277](#n277)</sup>. Organisers of a protest in Berlin said Bangladesh's authorities said they knew nothing until the media reported it <sup>[N260](#n260)</sup>. On 25 October 2026 parliament's foreign affairs committee, chaired by Zahurat Adib Chowdhury, asked the foreign ministry to report whether the Jeddah consulate was weak or negligent, what action was taken and what is being done to stop a repeat; the ministry has asked Saudi Arabia questions about the case <sup>[N85](#n85)</sup>.
- *The bodies:* the state minister said how the bodies could be brought back would be known only after Saudi Arabia answered Bangladesh's letter <sup>[N277](#n277)</sup>. The consulate's 19 October 2026 diplomatic note asked six questions, including where the bodies were, whether they would be sent to Bangladesh or had been buried in Saudi Arabia, and whether lawyers had been appointed for the two or either had asked for legal help; as of 24 October Saudi Arabia had not replied <sup>[N273](#n273)</sup>. Bangladesh also wrote asking for copies of the verdicts <sup>[N278](#n278)</sup>. The expatriates' welfare minister said the bodies would be brought home once air links and the overall situation normalised <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Were both executed Bangladeshis Al Majal cleaners <sup>[N259](#n259)</sup>, or was one an Umrah pilgrim, as the minister said?
> - Who decides whether an executed worker's body is sent home or buried in Saudi Arabia, and who pays?

<a id="if-something-goes-wrong"></a>

### 2.15 If something goes wrong

<a id="if-you-arent-paid"></a>

#### 2.15.1 If you aren't paid

File within 12 months <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>. You can claim unpaid wages, overtime, leave pay and end-of-service pay. Act in this order.

1. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-1"></a>**Collect proof:** your Qiwa contract, bank statements, payslips, messages about pay, and a list of missing months and amounts <sup>[N279](#n279), [9](#o9)</sup>. Sign nothing saying you were paid <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.

2. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-2"></a>**File a friendly-settlement claim with the labour ministry.** Every wage claim starts here; unsettled claims go to the labour court <sup>[9](#o9), [32 (2024)](#o32)</sup>. Claims recover money slowly, and wage-theft victims are rarely paid, sometimes only after years <sup>[N280](#n280)</sup>.
   You'll need <sup>[32 (2024)](#o32), [98 (2024)](#o98), [97](#o97)</sup>:

   - your iqama number;
   - a phone for SMS codes (a Bangladeshi number can be added);
   - the employer's name, your job title and start date;
   - the city where you last worked;
   - each claim with months and amounts;
   - proof as PDF, JPG, JPEG, TIF or PNG files up to 5 MB each.

   1. On the [Friendly Settlement service page](https://www.hrsd.gov.sa/en/ministry-services/services/269970) press Start Service and log in to the Individual Portal <sup>[97](#o97), [32 (2024)](#o32), [98 (2024)](#o98)</sup>.
   2. Choose Amicable Settlement Lawsuit Filing, check your details, pick your employer (or Another Establishment to type it), enter the job details and the settlement office for your city, add each claim with its amount, attach proof, tick the declaration and press Submit Claim <sup>[32 (2024)](#o32), [97](#o97)</sup>. Keep it short, with dates, amounts and a list of attachments <sup>[N279](#n279)</sup>. Template: "I worked for [company] as [job] from [date]. My contract wage is SR [amount] a month. I have not been paid for [months], a total of SR [amount]. I ask for these wages to be paid. Attached: contract, bank statements, messages." You get a claim number at once and the review result by SMS within 48 hours <sup>[98 (2024)](#o98)</sup>. Settlement is meant to take 21 working days <sup>[32 (2024)](#o32)</sup>.
   3. Join the session by Webex video call and show your iqama; there is no interpreter for non-Arabic speakers <sup>[98 (2024)](#o98)</sup>. The settlement service is offered in Arabic, English and Urdu <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>. If you miss it the claim is dismissed, but you can reopen it from My Claims within 21 working days <sup>[32 (2024)](#o32), [98 (2024)](#o98)</sup>. If the employer doesn't come, its services are suspended and a new date is set; if it keeps not coming, you can move employer without its consent and the claim goes to court <sup>[32 (2024)](#o32)</sup>. An attorney, including an embassy attorney, can file the claim for you with a power of attorney number <sup>[98 (2024)](#o98)</sup>.
   4. If settled, print the settlement report from My Lawsuits <sup>[32 (2024)](#o32)</sup>; if not, the claim goes to the labour court on Najiz, the Justice Ministry's online court system <sup>[9](#o9), [32 (2024)](#o32)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How does a foreign worker first create the Individual Portal login (Nafath or Absher)?
   > - Does filing cost SR 0 or SR 100 <sup>[97](#o97), [32 (2024)](#o32)</sup>?
   > - May an interpreter or the labour wing join the session?
   > - How do you enforce a settlement the employer ignores?

3. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-3"></a>**At the same time, ask the Bangladesh Embassy's labour wing for help.** It advises workers in disputes and goes to the labour court with them <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>. Call free on 8001000125 in Saudi Arabia or 0114195300 (extensions 401-403), or email counselorriyadh@probashi.gov.bd or labourwing.riyadh@yahoo.com <sup>[79](#o79)</sup>. Or use its [online service request form](https://www.bangladeshembassy.org.sa/srspub/): name, mobile, city, iqama and passport numbers, the problem type (for wages, the housing, food and wage one), a description in Bangla or English, and copies of your iqama and passport <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>. Template: "I am [name], iqama [number], working for [company] in [city]. I have not been paid since [month]. I have filed a friendly-settlement claim, number [number]. Please help me with the hearing." Office: 8039 Dareen Street, Diplomatic Quarter, Riyadh <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What are its office hours?
   > - What do you bring to a walk-in visit?
   > - How soon does it answer the form?
   > - How do you give it a power of attorney?

   In August 2025 the Expatriates' Welfare Ministry said it had appointed legal consultancy firms in Qatar, Oman, Saudi Arabia, the UAE and Kuwait <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which firm did the ministry appoint in Saudi Arabia, and how does a worker reach it?

4. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-4"></a>**From Bangladesh, a relative can ask the government to act.** Send the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare (Welfare-1 branch, saswelfare@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030251) a short application saying what happened and the wages, allowances or insurance owed, with <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>:

   - copies of your passport, iqama and BMET smart card;
   - your Saudi ID number;
   - the employer's name and full address.

   It is free, and within 7 working days the ministry asks the labour wing and the Wage Earners' Welfare Board to act <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>. Template: "My [brother], [name], passport [number], works for [company], [address], Saudi Arabia. He has not been paid since [month]; SR [amount] is owed. Copies attached. Please ask the labour wing in Riyadh to recover his wages." If nothing happens, contact the head of its Mission and Welfare wing: 02-41030219, 01711427769, adsmission@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>. OKUP (01842773300) gives free legal advice and helps families take cases <sup>[N281](#n281)</sup>.

5. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-5"></a>**If the whole company has stopped paying, claim wage insurance.** It pays only when the employer hasn't paid 80% or more of its workers for over 6 months, after friendly settlement <sup>[99 (2024)](#o99)</sup>: up to 6 months' unpaid wages at your Qiwa contract rate and up to SR 1,000 for a ticket home, at most SR 17,500, not end-of-service pay <sup>[99 (2024)](#o99)</sup>. You can claim in Saudi Arabia, even after changing employer <sup>[99 (2024)](#o99)</sup>; domestic, seasonal, temporary and farm workers aren't covered <sup>[99 (2024)](#o99)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[99 (2024)](#o99)</sup>:

   - the claim form and declaration;
   - proof of the last wage received;
   - your contract;
   - the official document showing wages owed;
   - your iqama copy;
   - your final exit visa, for the ticket.

   1. Check your cover with your iqama number at [labor.aletihad.sa](https://labor.aletihad.sa/), run by the insurer Al-Etihad Cooperative Insurance (free phone 800 3040 110) <sup>[99 (2024)](#o99), [100](#o100)</sup>.
   2. Claim in its Worker Portal (Submit Claim) with your iqama or border number and mobile, saying whether you have left Saudi Arabia <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.
   3. You or a legal representative send the documents listed under You'll need above <sup>[99 (2024)](#o99)</sup>.
   4. The employer has 10 working days to object <sup>[99 (2024)](#o99)</sup>.

   Most wage-theft cases fall outside these rules <sup>[N280](#n280)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What do you do if the claim is refused?

6. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-6"></a>**After 3 months without pay you may change employer without consent** (see [If you want to change employer](#if-you-want-to-change-employer)) <sup>[N140](#n140)</sup>.

<a id="if-your-employer-reports-you-absent-huroob"></a>

#### 2.15.2 If your employer reports you absent (huroob)

Huroob ("running away") is a Qiwa report that you stopped coming to work: your status becomes "Absent from Work", the report can't be withdrawn, and you are removed from the company's records <sup>[31 (2022)](#o31)</sup>. You can be reported absent in two ways:

- **The employer files it.** It can't while you have an active contract recorded on Qiwa, so make sure yours is recorded. It can file only once there is no active Qiwa contract (none was recorded, or it has ended), your permit or iqama has at least 60 days left, and you have been absent over 30 days in a contract year or 15 in a row <sup>[31 (2022)](#o31), [N282](#n282)</sup>.
- **Qiwa files it automatically.** When your Qiwa contract ends you get 60 days to move, leave or sign again; after that Qiwa marks you absent without any report from the employer <sup>[N282](#n282), [N283](#n283)</sup>.

These are the rules for company workers on Qiwa; for a domestic worker, see [Work visa: domestic worker](#work-visa-domestic-worker), If something goes wrong.

1. <a id="if-your-employer-reports-you-absent-huroob-item-1"></a>**Check your status often** on your Absher dashboard ("Absent from Work" or blocked services) <sup>[N202](#n202)</sup> or, with your iqama number or passport number and nationality, on the ministry's [expatriate employee inquiry](https://es.hrsd.gov.sa/services/inquiry/nonsaudiempinquiry.aspx) <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>. The SMS alert may not arrive if your number isn't linked to the government system <sup>[31 (2022)](#o31), [N127 (2024)](#n127)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-your-employer-reports-you-absent-huroob-item-2"></a>**Within 60 days, move or leave:** transfer on Qiwa if you have been in Saudi Arabia over 12 months, or issue yourself a final exit visa on Absher and go home <sup>[31 (2022)](#o31), [N284](#n284)</sup>. Do nothing and you stay "absent" and illegal, and leaving that way brings a 3-5 year re-entry ban <sup>[31 (2022)](#o31), [N151](#n151)</sup>. A final exit visa needs <sup>[29](#o29)</sup>:

   - you in Saudi Arabia;
   - a valid iqama;
   - a passport valid at least 60 days;
   - traffic fines paid;
   - fingerprints registered;
   - no vehicle in your name.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What is the Absher menu path for a worker's own final exit visa?
   > - What is the fee for a worker's own final exit visa?
   > - How long is a worker's own final exit visa valid?

3. <a id="if-your-employer-reports-you-absent-huroob-item-3"></a>**After 60 days,** if you have been in Saudi Arabia over 12 months, a new employer can still move you on Qiwa, paying overdue permit fees <sup>[N283](#n283), [N285](#n285)</sup>; otherwise apply to the embassy's Special Exit Programme (who it covers and how: see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers)) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-your-employer-reports-you-absent-huroob-item-4"></a>**If the report is false:** employers file false huroob reports to exploit workers; the burden of proof is on you, and some workers are asked for bribes to withdraw it <sup>[N150 (2022)](#n150)</sup>. Tell the labour wing on its online form (see [If you aren't paid, item 3: At the same time, ask the Bangladesh Embassy's labour wing for help](#if-you-arent-paid-item-3)), choosing the huroob problem type, or the court or jail type if you are also accused of a crime <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Can friendly settlement or the labour court cancel a false report?
   > - How do you answer a false theft charge?

Anyone offering to remove a huroob for a fee is a scammer; a real change shows only on Absher, Qiwa or the ministry's site <sup>[N202](#n202)</sup>.

<a id="where-to-complain"></a>

#### 2.15.3 Where to complain

Which route takes which problem:

- **A right broken at work** (hours, heat, housing, leave): report it in the labour ministry's (Ministry of Human Resources and Social Development) app, on Google Play and the App Store, or in Tawakkalna, the Saudi government app: service Reporting Violations of Labor Regulations, sign in with Absher, pick the report type, give the workplace location, company details and proof; it is free, the ministry acts within 3 days and the status shows in the app <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.
- **Money owed:** friendly settlement ([If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)) <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.
- **Help from Bangladesh's own officials:** the embassy labour wing, free on 8001000125 or through its online form ([If you aren't paid, item 3: At the same time, ask the Bangladesh Embassy's labour wing for help](#if-you-arent-paid-item-3)) <sup>[79](#o79), [55](#o55)</sup>.
- **No iqama, or questions about your rights:** the ministry help line 19911, Sunday to Thursday 8 am to 5 pm, Arabic and English <sup>[102 (2024)](#o102), [23](#o23)</sup>.
- **No health insurance, or the premium taken from your pay:** care.chi.gov.sa (see Housing, food and health care) <sup>[87 (2024)](#o87)</sup>.
- **Passport or iqama kept by the employer:** the Beneficiary Page (see [Your passport](#your-passport-1)) <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.
- **A false absence report:** see ["If your employer reports you absent (huroob)"](#if-your-employer-reports-you-absent-huroob).
- **A woman who needs a safe place to stay:** the labour wings run safe homes for women in Riyadh, Jeddah and Madinah, with the Wage Earners' Welfare Board paying staff, food and treatment; a worker or her family applies, free, through the district administration's probashi kallyan (expatriate welfare) branch, the DEMO or directly to the Board, which writes to the embassy <sup>[103 (2024)](#o103), [76](#o76)</sup>. In September 2026 the state minister for expatriates' welfare, Md Nurul Haque, said a safe home runs in Saudi Arabia <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>, and in the same month parliament was told there are two safe homes in Saudi Arabia <sup>[N83](#n83)</sup>. On the Saudi side, the government had tasked an organisation called Sakan with housing and legal help for foreign workers in difficulty, stopped its funding in 2023, and now has another organisation, Samasco, do this work, the labour ministry's spokesperson told the New York Times; three Kenyan women in a Samasco shelter said they would not be allowed home unless each paid USD 400, and Samasco did not respond <sup>[N117](#n117)</sup>.

**Inspections.** In May 2024 an assistant deputy minister at the labour ministry, Faisal Al-Dafayan, told the Bangladesh embassy that the ministry had recently appointed 1,200 labour inspectors to act on workers' complaints, and that they resolved about 10 lakh complaints in 2023 <sup>[N74 (2024)](#n74)</sup>.

**The bilateral agreement.** Ali Haider Chowdhury, former secretary general of BAIRA (the Bangladesh Association of International Recruiting Agencies), said in October 2025 that workers are usually sent under memoranda of understanding with no legal obligation, while the agreement gives workers sent to Saudi Arabia legal protection and lets the government go to court for a worker if needed <sup>[N99](#n99)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has the Bangladesh government ever gone to a Saudi court for a worker under the agreement, and how does a worker ask it to?

<a id="if-an-agency-broker-or-relative-takes-your-money"></a>

#### 2.15.4 If an agency, broker or relative takes your money

**If the agency takes your money and doesn't send you, sends you to a different job or overcharges, complain to BMET.** It is free and meant to take 30 working days: an inquiry committee hears both sides, BMET recommends, the ministry decides <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>. It recovers at most the official cost of the job, Tk 1.65 lakh, not what you paid <sup>[N8](#n8), [N188 (2019)](#n188)</sup>. In practice many complaints stay unresolved for years <sup>[N189 (2024)](#n189)</sup>; in one study of BMET complaints only 39% were resolved, typically with Tk 20,000 to just over Tk 1 lakh <sup>[N154](#n154)</sup>.

You'll need:

- your name, NID number, passport number, BMET smart card number if you have one, age, father's or husband's and mother's names, permanent address and phone <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>;
- if abroad, your address there and a relative (name, relation, mobile) to attend for you <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>;
- the agency's name, RL number and address, or the broker's name, age, parents' names and address <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>;
- any earlier complaints against them <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>;
- proof such as receipts and the contract <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.

1. <a id="if-an-agency-broker-or-relative-takes-your-money-item-1"></a>**File online** on OEP's complaint system (BMET's "online complaint" link goes to employee.oep.gov.bd); you or your family can upload documents and follow progress <sup>[N178](#n178), [104](#o104)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-an-agency-broker-or-relative-takes-your-money-item-2"></a>**Or on paper:** fill in BMET's [complaint form (Form Ka)](https://objectstorage.ap-dcc-gazipur-1.oraclecloud15.com/n/axvjbnqprylg/b/V2Ministry/o/office-bmet/2024/12/fe647f3d68c14792a15f496990376a8a.PDF) with the reason and what happened, sign or thumbprint it, and hand it with copies of your proof to BMET (89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000), your DEMO, or the Bangladesh Embassy's labour wing if abroad <sup>[43](#o43), [21](#o21)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-an-agency-broker-or-relative-takes-your-money-item-3"></a>**No action in 30 working days:** write to BMET's complaint officer, the Director (Employment), diremi@bmet.gov.bd, 01711111544, who has 3 months <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-an-agency-broker-or-relative-takes-your-money-item-4"></a>**Then** appeal to the Secretary, Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, secretary@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030444 (+880 2 41030444 from abroad), who has 1 month; then [grs.gov.bd](https://www.grs.gov.bd/), 3 months <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
5. <a id="if-an-agency-broker-or-relative-takes-your-money-item-5"></a>**Or go to the ministry directly:** its Enforcement-2 branch, sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd or 02-41030255, with an application, proof, your full address and the agency's name, RL number and address; free, action within 3-5 working days, settlement meant within 30 working days <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>. Hotline 16135, or +880 9610102030 from abroad <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>.

**Free legal help:** OKUP (Ovibashi Karmi Unnayan Program, a migrant workers' organisation), 01842773300, gives legal advice and helps file a case <sup>[N281](#n281)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the menu path to OEP's complaint form?
> - Does a paper complaint get a receipt or complaint number?
> - How do you follow up a paper complaint?
> - Does IOM in Dhaka take up individual complaints (the ILO doesn't <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>)?

**If the relative cheats you, call the government legal aid helpline 16699 before going to the thana (police station)** <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>. The law reaches the relative, but it can reach you too. Section 31 of the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act 2013 (OEMA) makes it a crime for any person, not only an agency, to take money with a false promise of a job abroad or to keep a migrant's passport or visa: up to 5 years and a fine of at least Tk 1 lakh <sup>[107 (2013)](#o107)</sup>. These offences are cognisable, non-bailable and can't be settled privately <sup>[108 (2013)](#o108)</sup>. But section 33 makes buying or selling a visa inside Bangladesh an offence for any person, 2 to 7 years' rigorous imprisonment and at least Tk 3 lakh, so a worker who paid for a visa can be accused too, and the seller will say so <sup>[109](#o109)</sup>.

All of these routes can run at once:

- **Free legal help first.** OEMA gives people cheated in the name of migration a right to legal aid <sup>[110 (2013)](#o110)</sup>. Call the government legal aid helpline 16699 (from abroad +8809612316699) <sup>[106](#o106), [111](#o111)</sup>, or apply at the District Legal Aid Office in the district judge court building <sup>[N286 (2023)](#n286)</sup>, which also mediates fraud and money disputes free for people earning up to Tk 1 lakh a year <sup>[N286 (2023)](#n286)</sup>. OKUP, 01842773300, helps families file cases <sup>[N281](#n281)</sup>.
- **Name the people in Bangladesh who took the money.** When the relative lives in Saudi Arabia, his family at home usually received the payment, as with the Banshkhali man's wife and the Bishwanath family <sup>[N200](#n200), [N195](#n195)</sup>. Complain against each person named on the bank and bKash records, not only the man abroad <sup>[N200](#n200)</sup>.
- **Village salish (সালিস) or the Union Parishad chairman.** Most families go here first, and it rarely returns the money: in Natore a salish ordered the man to repay Tk 1 lakh to each family, a fraction of what they paid, and he ignored it; in Bishwanath the accused ignored three notices, and the chairman certified the victims to go to court <sup>[N208 (2023)](#n208), [N195](#n195)</sup>. BRAC's migration programme runs social arbitration instead, where volunteers and local leaders press the broker to repay <sup>[N287](#n287)</sup>.
- **Village court (গ্রাম আদালত)** at the Union Parishad, for money owed under an agreement, receipt or other document, up to Tk 3 lakh since 2024: apply on Form 1 with a Tk 20 fee <sup>[112 (2024)](#o112), [N288](#n288)</sup>.
- **Complain to BMET or the ministry against the relative by name.** Section 41 lets anyone harmed complain against any person, not only an agency; the inquiry is meant to take 30 working days, and section 43 lets the government recover the money and pay it to you <sup>[113 (2013)](#o113), [114 (2013)](#o114)</sup>. Use the agency complaint route (["If an agency, broker or relative takes your money"](#if-an-agency-broker-or-relative-takes-your-money), items 1-5), giving the person's name, age, parents' names and address <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>. BMET settled 2,054 complaints and recovered nearly Tk 9.92 crore in 2025 <sup>[N289](#n289)</sup>, but it pays on the official cost of the job, the legal maximum of Tk 1.65 lakh (see [The legal maximum and what people really pay](#the-legal-maximum-and-what-people-really-pay)), rather than what you paid, and can't enforce its decision <sup>[N8](#n8), [N188 (2019)](#n188)</sup>.
- **Police case (FIR) at the thana** under OEMA section 31, or under the human trafficking law if the person was sold, confined or forced to work <sup>[N206](#n206)</sup>. Expect resistance: in Natore the officer in charge refused nine complaints, saying the men hadn't gone abroad legally so the money wasn't the police's job <sup>[N208 (2023)](#n208)</sup>.
- **If the police won't register it, file a complaint case (সি.আর. মামলা, CR case) at the magistrate's court,** as the Bishwanath victims did at the Senior Judicial Magistrate's court in Sylhet <sup>[N195](#n195)</sup>. A first-class judicial magistrate tries OEMA offences, and the trial is meant to end within 4 months of charges, extendable by 2 <sup>[115 (2013)](#o115)</sup>. You can sue for compensation in a civil court alongside <sup>[116 (2013)](#o116)</sup>.
- **Or file at the Human Trafficking Offence Suppression Tribunal,** where the person was sold, confined or forced to work. Families have filed there directly: in two October 2024 Barishal cases the judge ordered the local police station's officer in charge to record the complaint as an FIR, and in the SJ Group case also ordered an investigation report <sup>[N239 (2024)](#n239)</sup>; in April 2025 the Rajbari tribunal ordered PBI (the Police Bureau of Investigation) Faridpur to investigate a wife's case <sup>[N290](#n290)</sup>.

Relatives, friends and neighbours with connections are the people who most often cheat migrants, which is why victims avoid the courts: in a survey of 5,400 households in Kalihati, Tangail, 40% of migrant households had been defrauded and 19% had paid and never gone <sup>[N188 (2019)](#n188)</sup>.

**If the relative lives in Saudi Arabia,** a case against him alone stalls: a Cumilla broker's case against the friend in Saudi Arabia who supplied his visas went nowhere, and in Natore the salish happened only after the man came home and the families went to his house <sup>[N43 (2024)](#n43), [N208 (2023)](#n208)</sup>.

**Cases**

- 2002 to 2012: Lal Mia (54) of Shalikha, Magura, said he paid a broker Tk 1.3 lakh in 2002 to go to Saudi Arabia but could not go, then paid brokers Tk 2.5 lakh for Malaysia in 2006, Tk 3 lakh for Malaysia in 2010 and Tk 3.6 lakh for Dubai in 2012, about Tk 10.5 lakh over the four attempts, selling four bighas of farmland and 12.5 decimals of homestead; he never went abroad or got the money back <sup>[N291](#n291)</sup>.
- March 2024: the family of cousins Sohel Molla and Iqbal Molla of Jaldia village, Sultanpur union, Rajbari Sadar paid neighbour Moniruddin Molla and his brother-in-law Mamun Molla Tk 6 lakh in cash on 5 March 2024 and Tk 4 lakh through a bank on 26 May 2024 for good jobs in Saudi Arabia, where, the family says, the two got no work and were abused by the broker ring <sup>[N290](#n290)</sup>. Sohel's father, Kamal Molla, said the brokers took Tk 2 lakh more on 7 February 2025 on a promise of work, after which the men lost contact with their families; in May 2025 Sohel's wife, Rina Akter, said the brokers were demanding Tk 10 lakh more to bring them back <sup>[N290](#n290)</sup>. On 27 April 2025 she filed a case under the human trafficking law against Moniruddin and Mamun at Rajbari's human trafficking offences tribunal, which ordered PBI Faridpur to investigate; Mamun said the men had refused to work at the company they were given and their passports were returned from the company through the agency <sup>[N290](#n290)</sup>.
- June 2025: according to the CID (Criminal Investigation Department of the Bangladesh police), a ring took Tk 6 lakh from a man for a fast-food shop job in Saudi Arabia, sent him there on 7 June 2025 with no job, held him in a house, beat him and demanded more money from his family for an iqama; he escaped, was detained by Saudi police and sent back on 22 July 2026, and his wife filed a human trafficking case at Savar police station <sup>[N292](#n292)</sup>. The CID's human trafficking unit arrested ring member Md Salman Hossain (33) at Dhaka's Shahjalal airport on 31 August 2026; the CID said the victim had been held in Salman's own house in Saudi Arabia <sup>[N292](#n292)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has any family recovered visa money from a relative through BRAC's arbitration, a village court, a police case or BMET?
> - If so, how much did they recover and how long did it take?
> - Does the labour wing in Riyadh or Jeddah act on a complaint against a Bangladeshi in Saudi Arabia who took visa or iqama money?

<a id="changing-employer-or-leaving"></a>

### 2.16 Changing employer or leaving

<a id="if-you-want-to-change-employer"></a>

#### 2.16.1 If you want to change employer

**You can move without your employer's consent:**

- when your Qiwa contract has ended <sup>[27 (2020)](#o27)</sup>;
- after 12 months' work since entering Saudi Arabia, with 90 days' notice (less if the employer agrees) and a valid contract on Qiwa <sup>[27 (2020)](#o27), [N140](#n140)</sup>;
- at any time if you haven't been paid for 3 months in a row, your work permit or iqama has expired, the employer has disappeared (travel, prison, death), or you report that the business is secretly run by a foreigner in a Saudi's name and you weren't part of it <sup>[N140](#n140)</sup>;
- if the employer keeps missing your friendly-settlement sessions <sup>[32 (2024)](#o32)</sup>.

Your employer is only notified and can't refuse <sup>[N140](#n140)</sup>. These are the company-worker rules; a domestic worker's routes are different (see [Work visa: domestic worker](#work-visa-domestic-worker); see [Domestic worker to another household](#domestic-worker-to-another-household); see [Domestic worker to a company job](#domestic-worker-to-a-company-job)) <sup>[30 (2024)](#o30), [32 (2024)](#o32), [N293 (2022)](#n293)</sup>.

1. <a id="if-you-want-to-change-employer-item-1"></a>**Find a new employer yourself;** workers go to industrial areas and apply at every factory <sup>[N140](#n140)</sup>. Check the company (see [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), Before you pay anyone). Pay no one for a job offer or transfer; the new employer pays the fees <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does any official service match foreign workers already in Saudi Arabia with employers?

2. <a id="if-you-want-to-change-employer-item-2"></a>**Accept its offer on Qiwa:** log in with Nafath, open the offer, check the job title and wage, accept or reject <sup>[N294](#n294), [117](#o117)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-want-to-change-employer-item-3"></a>**Your employer is notified and your notice runs,** unless waived <sup>[117](#o117), [N140](#n140)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-you-want-to-change-employer-item-4"></a>**The new employer completes the move;** once approved, the new work permit is issued at once, at no cost to you <sup>[117](#o117), [9](#o9), [N140](#n140)</sup>.

Keep working for your current employer until Qiwa shows the move is done, or you may never get a permit and stay illegal <sup>[N127 (2024)](#n127)</sup>. Never share your Absher or Qiwa password or SMS codes, even with your employer <sup>[N127 (2024)](#n127)</sup>. Some employers block moves by cancelling the iqama with a final exit visa or accusing the worker of theft or debt, and some new employers wrongly ask for the old employer's approval <sup>[N127 (2024)](#n127), [N140](#n140)</sup>; if blocked, file friendly settlement <sup>[N285](#n285)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do you do if the transfer is rejected or never completed?

<a id="if-you-resign-or-are-dismissed"></a>

#### 2.16.2 If you resign or are dismissed

You can resign before a fixed-term contract ends, and since 2025 the employer can't claim compensation from you <sup>[N144 (2024)](#n144)</sup>.

**Notice if you resign.** A resignation counts as accepted 30 days after you hand it in if the employer doesn't answer; the employer can delay it up to 60 days, and you can withdraw it within 7 days <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.

**Notice at the end of a contract.** To leave when the contract ends, give notice 30 days before a fixed-term contract ends, or 60 days on an open-ended one <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.

**If the employer dismisses you.** If the employer ends the contract without a valid reason, you are owed 15 days' wages per year of service on an open-ended contract or the wages for the rest of a fixed-term one, and never less than 2 months' wages <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.

**What you pay if you leave early.** The employer pays your ticket home, also when it ends the contract in a trial period; you pay it only if you are unfit for the work or quit without a valid reason <sup>[35](#o35), [9](#o9)</sup>. For what you are still owed, see [Leaving at the end of the job, item 4: Collect what you are owed](#leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-4).

**Leaving without notice**
You may stop work without notice and keep all your rights if the employer doesn't meet its duties (for example, doesn't pay), moves you to another job without consent, assaults or mistreats you, leaves a known serious danger unfixed, or lied about the job when you signed <sup>[9](#o9), [50 (2023)](#o50)</sup>. If you just stop coming to work, the employer can report you absent (see ["If your employer reports you absent (huroob)"](#if-your-employer-reports-you-absent-huroob)) <sup>[31 (2022)](#o31)</sup> and, after a written warning, dismiss you without end-of-service pay for over 30 days' absence in a contract year or 15 in a row <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.

<a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job"></a>

#### 2.16.3 Leaving at the end of the job

1. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-1"></a>**Check for a travel ban before you book.** An unpaid loan, credit card, rent or a cheque given as security can bring a court travel ban of up to 3 years per order <sup>[N295](#n295)</sup>. A ban shows in Absher's travel ban enquiry, and the debt case on Najiz, the Justice Ministry's portal (log in with Nafath) <sup>[N295](#n295)</sup>.
