2. <a id="help-from-bangladesh-item-2"></a>**Send an application** describing what happened, with these papers, to the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, Welfare-2 branch (aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd) <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>.
3. <a id="help-from-bangladesh-item-3"></a>**If nothing happens within 7 working days,** write to the head of its Mission and Welfare wing (adsmission@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030219, 01711427769) <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>.

<a id="amnesties-and-grace-periods"></a>

### 15.2 Amnesties and grace periods

The last known window to leave without a fine ended on 18 April 2026 <sup>[N162](#n162)</sup>. In it, people whose visit, Umrah or final exit visa expired on 25 February 2026 could leave without a fine by going to the airport with a ticket <sup>[N162](#n162)</sup>; the embassy's notice of 23 March 2026 listed every kind of visit visa, Umrah, transit and final exit visas past their validity on 25 February, and warned that anyone not leaving by 18 April could face serious legal trouble and punishment <sup>[N350](#n350)</sup>. The embassy announces such windows on [bangladeshembassy.org.sa/notice.html](https://www.bangladeshembassy.org.sa/notice.html); check it for a new one <sup>[N162](#n162), [153](#o153)</sup>.

Separately, until the end of 2026 an employer can regularise a work permit that expired more than 12 months ago or was never issued within 6 months of joining; cases not regularised then face legal measures <sup>[N163](#n163)</sup>. On 30 June 2026 the embassy relayed this labour ministry call on its verified Facebook page: work permits (rukhsa amal) expired over 12 months, or not issued over 6 months after entry or transfer, must be issued or renewed within 2026, or legal action follows <sup>[N351](#n351)</sup>.

Bangladesh's expatriates' welfare adviser said on 31 May 2025 that Saudi Arabia had assured him it would regularise irregular Bangladeshi workers and had already started <sup>[N352](#n352)</sup>. A working team of officials from the Saudi labour ministry (the Ministry of Human Resources and Social Development) and the Bangladesh embassy, promised at a meeting on 30 January 2025, was formed to solve specific problems of irregular Bangladeshi workers, and a high-powered task force on illegal workers and recruitment irregularities had been formed under the two countries' latest foreign office consultations, according to a press release reported in May 2025 <sup>[N78](#n78)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is any amnesty for overstayers open now or announced?
> - How does a worker check whether their case qualifies for regularisation?
> - What can a worker do if the employer won't regularise the work permit?
> - What has the 2025 Saudi-Bangladesh working team on irregular workers delivered, and can a worker bring a case to it?
> - Was the regularisation the adviser announced in May 2025 the employer-led work-permit scheme above, or a separate one, and how does a worker apply?

<a id="if-you-are-detained"></a>

### 15.3 If you are detained

People picked up in raids have usually been held one to two weeks in a deportation centre, then flown straight home, often with nothing <sup>[N160 (2024)](#n160)</sup>. Jabbar, a worker without papers who believed the police would send him home if they caught him, gave himself up; they took his fingerprints and released him, leaving him with no money for food, his wife said (May 2025) <sup>[N164](#n164)</sup>. Embassy officials visit the centres and issue your travel permit there <sup>[N349](#n349)</sup>; for legal help or to tell your family, see [Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), If you or a relative is arrested <sup>[55](#o55), [79](#o79)</sup>. Wages owed can still be claimed after you leave: wage insurance if the whole company stopped paying (see [If you aren't paid, item 5: If the whole company has stopped paying, claim wage insurance](#if-you-arent-paid-item-5)), or a relative's application to the ministry's Welfare-1 branch (see [If you aren't paid, item 4: From Bangladesh, a relative can ask the government to act](#if-you-arent-paid-item-4)) <sup>[100](#o100), [69](#o69)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a friendly-settlement claim be filed, or continued, from Bangladesh after deportation?

Embassy efforts freed 49,503 Bangladeshis from 20 countries in 2012-2016, 17,335 of them from Saudi Arabia, most of them workers whose iqama or visa had expired, for whom "release" meant arranging an air ticket and an outpass (travel permit), according to a Prothom Alo column <sup>[N275](#n275)</sup>. In October 2011 a Dammam high court asked what the Bangladesh government was doing to bring home about 500 Bangladeshis held in Dammam prison whom Saudi Arabia was willing to send home under a general pardon; tickets and outpasses weren't arranged because of embassy indifference and errors in the paperwork, according to the same column <sup>[N275](#n275)</sup>.

<a id="if-a-worker-dies-is-injured-or-loses-the-job"></a>

## 16 If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job

<a id="if-a-worker-dies-in-saudi-arabia"></a>

### 16.1 If a worker dies in Saudi Arabia

1. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-in-saudi-arabia-item-1"></a>**Call the embassy:** +966570212180, or the labour wing free on 8001000125 <sup>[N300](#n300), [79](#o79)</sup>. It registers the death, follows up with the employer and the authorities, and issues the no-objection certificate (NOC) needed for burial or repatriation <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>.

   The cause of death is recorded in Saudi Arabia, usually as "natural", and Bangladesh does not check it (see [The recorded cause of death](#the-recorded-cause-of-death)) <sup>[N161](#n161)</sup>. If the worker had no valid papers, bringing the body home takes much longer <sup>[N161](#n161)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How can a family ask for a post-mortem in Saudi Arabia?
   > - How can a family challenge the recorded cause of death?

2. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-in-saudi-arabia-item-2"></a>**Ask Bangladesh to bring the body home, or say you want burial in Saudi Arabia.** Under Saudi rules the employer pays to prepare and fly the body home unless the family agrees to burial there <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>. The assistant director of the Faridpur Probashi Kallyan (expatriate welfare) Centre said in June 2026 that the company usually sends the body home and gives other help, and that if it won't, the government brings the body home on the family's application <sup>[N353](#n353)</sup>. On the Bangladesh side, the route depends on the worker's papers:

   - **The worker left with BMET clearance or joined the Welfare Board through the embassy:** tell the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Eskaton Garden, Dhaka) whether the family wants the body brought home or buried in Saudi Arabia <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>. The Board pays to bring the body home, relays the family's wish to the embassy and follows up until the body arrives, free <sup>[N161](#n161), [76](#o76)</sup>. A district welfare centre also takes written applications: in March 2025 the Mymensingh centre's assistant director said it would forward a father's written application to the Board, which would then make all arrangements <sup>[N126](#n126)</sup>.
   - **The worker had neither, including workers without valid papers:** the family can still apply to the Welfare Board, which asks the ministry to pay <sup>[N161](#n161)</sup>. The ministry's Welfare-2 branch (aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd) also takes applications by email for irregular, undocumented and destitute workers under a 2022 policy; free, action within 7 working days <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>. **You'll need** <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>:
     - a description;
     - the cause of death;
     - the death certificate;
     - copies of the worker's passport, work permit or iqama, BMET smart card and Saudi ID number.

     For a worker who died without papers, the Dohar UNO (upazila nirbahi officer, the upazila's chief government officer) said in April 2026 that if someone contacts the embassy in Riyadh on the worker's behalf, it will arrange to send the body home with government help <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - When does the Board or the ministry pay to bring the body home instead of the employer?
   > - For a worker without BMET clearance, should the family apply to the Welfare Board, to the Welfare-2 branch, or both?
   > - How does the family apply to the Welfare Board to have the body brought home: in person, by email or on its portal?
   > - Which papers does the Board need from the family?
   > - How long does the Board take to get the body home?

   BRAC, a Bangladeshi development organisation, helps families apply through its Migration Programme ([At Dhaka airport](#at-dhaka-airport)) <sup>[N354](#n354), [N161](#n161)</sup>. Union Parishad (local council) chairmen and UNOs have also helped families gather the papers and reach the Board or the ministry <sup>[N265](#n265), [N266](#n266), [N355](#n355), [N356](#n356)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-in-saudi-arabia-item-3"></a>**At the airport,** the Board's desk calls the family, hands over the body at cargo with a Tk 35,000 cheque for transport and burial, and its ambulance carries the body from Dhaka, Chattogram or Sylhet airport, free <sup>[1](#o1), [76](#o76)</sup>. **You'll need** <sup>[76](#o76), [1](#o1)</sup>:
   - the worker's passport copy;
   - death certificate;
   - NOC;
   - original air waybill;
   - a heirship certificate from the Union Parishad (local council) chairman or ward councillor;
   - the wife's, father's or mother's NID;
   - any BMET clearance card;
   - a power of attorney if someone else collects.

   The arrival time can also reach the family through the local administration: the embassy's labour counsellor or the ministry writes to the deputy commissioner, the UNO and the expatriate-welfare office with the flight time, and they tell the family <sup>[N357](#n357), [N30](#n30)</sup>. The ministry said one body in October 2026 would be received at cargo gate 8 of Hazrat Shahjalal International Airport, Dhaka <sup>[N23](#n23)</sup>.

<a id="the-recorded-cause-of-death"></a>

#### 16.1.1 The recorded cause of death

- **Mostly "natural".** Bangladeshi authorities, working from Saudi papers, recorded 76% of deaths in Saudi Arabia from January to October 2022 as natural, and the average age of those "natural" deaths in 2022 was 44 <sup>[N358 (2024)](#n358)</sup>. Across all destinations, the papers with returned bodies gave natural death in 66% of cases in January to September 2026, against about 70% before, by the Expatriates' Welfare Ministry's data <sup>[N245](#n245)</sup>.
- **Mostly unexplained.** The Guardian found in March 2024 that in most cases the cause was not explained: Saudi death certificates give "natural causes", "heart problems" or "respiratory problems", so no post-mortem is done; FairSquare, a UK rights group, says the cause is unexplained in over half of migrant deaths in the Gulf <sup>[N358 (2024)](#n358)</sup>.
- **The Saudi position.** Saudi Arabia's labour ministry said in March 2024 that post-mortems are done where needed, death certificates are issued transparently, and it had found no negligence <sup>[N358 (2024)](#n358)</sup>.
- **Suicide.** Once the destination country sends a death certificate saying "suicide", the family can do nothing more about it, OKUP's chairperson said in January 2024 <sup>[N359 (2024)](#n359)</sup>. The bodies of 799 women migrant workers came home in eight years, most from Saudi Arabia, and many death certificates gave suicide as the cause, according to Welfare Board data cited in October 2026 <sup>[N83](#n83)</sup>.
- **Signs of violence.** In cases the New York Times investigated in 2025, Saudi authorities declared foreign domestic workers' deaths natural despite signs of violence: Ugandan Aisha Meeme's Saudi autopsy found three broken ribs and severe electrical burns, and her husband said the employer offered him the choice of her body or her USD 2,800 in unpaid wages <sup>[N117](#n117)</sup>.
- **Suspected causes.** Rights groups told the Guardian in 2024 that hard labour, living conditions, exploitation, stress and heat stroke may be driving the death rate; Shariful Hasan, associate director of BRAC's Migration Programme, said that since workers go only after passing a medical at a Saudi-designated centre, both countries must investigate why they die <sup>[N358 (2024)](#n358)</sup>. More than 16% of returned bodies in January to September 2026 died in road or work accidents, fires, falls from buildings or electrocution, and the minister said on 1 October 2026 that steps to verify causes of death abroad would be decided <sup>[N245](#n245)</sup>.

<a id="how-many-die"></a>

#### 16.1.2 How many die

At least 13,685 Bangladeshi workers died in Saudi Arabia in 2008-2022, 1,502 of them in 2022, more than four a day, by Bangladesh government data reported by the Guardian <sup>[N358 (2024)](#n358)</sup>. About 2,000 died there in 2026 up to the end of September, more than in any other country; the head of BRAC's Migration Programme said over 70% of bodies come from the Middle East, most from Saudi Arabia, and that young men aged 25 to 30 are also dying <sup>[N245](#n245)</sup>. From the start of the 2026 war to 12 October 2026, 14 Bangladeshis were killed in attacks in the Middle East, 8 of them registered as migrant workers, by Expatriates' Welfare Ministry sources <sup>[N31](#n31)</sup>.

<a id="if-the-worker-was-executed"></a>

#### 16.1.3 If the worker was executed

Saudi law doesn't guarantee that the body of an executed person is handed over: in all three executions of January-June 2025 among the cases Amnesty International documented, Saudi Arabia neither returned the body nor told the family where the grave is <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>. Asked in October 2026 whether the families of two executed Bangladeshis could get compensation, the state minister for foreign affairs said there was no chance because the sentence had already been carried out <sup>[N360](#n360)</sup>.

<a id="the-riyadh-sofa-factory-fire"></a>

#### 16.1.4 The Riyadh sofa-factory fire

- **9 August 2026:** a fire at a sofa factory in Riyadh killed Bangladeshi workers; the first night's reports put the toll at 10, by the Naogaon deputy commissioner's count, and the expatriates' welfare minister said officials had been told to bring the bodies home quickly and secure all government help and compensation for the families <sup>[N361](#n361)</sup>.
- **10 August:** the Foreign Ministry said embassy officials went to the site at once and worked with the Saudi authorities on identifying the dead, and that once identification was complete the bodies would be brought home in line with the families' consent; the Naogaon deputy commissioner said the embassy's labour welfare wing was identifying the victims and the process might take some days <sup>[N169](#n169), [N122](#n122)</sup>.
- **11 August:** the Prime Minister directed the Foreign Ministry, the Expatriates' Welfare Ministry and the embassy to bring the bodies of the 16 victims home in the shortest possible time <sup>[N362](#n362)</sup>.
- **26-27 August:** 9 bodies identified by fingerprints were flown home on Biman flight BG-340 and received at Shahjalal airport at about 4:30 pm on 27 August, with one relative from each family present, then taken home by vehicle; the 7 others were still being identified by DNA test <sup>[N357](#n357), [N363](#n363)</sup>. The labour counsellor in Riyadh had sent the Atrai upazila administration a signed letter with the arrival date, and the upazila told the families to receive the bodies at the airport <sup>[N357](#n357)</sup>.
- **28 August:** Shyamal Uddin Majhi (46) of Bagmara, Rajshahi, who had gone eight years earlier on borrowed money, was brought home; his coffin was opened at home in the presence of police, and his sister was worried about how his debt would be repaid <sup>[N171](#n171)</sup>. Families of the 7 still unidentified demanded quick identification; DNA tests were being used for the remaining Naogaon victims, and two Naldanga victims couldn't be brought home until identified <sup>[N171](#n171)</sup>.
- **31 October:** the 7 bodies, burned beyond recognition and identified by DNA, landed in Dhaka at about 8:30 am, 2 months 22 days after the deaths <sup>[N30](#n30), [N139](#n139)</sup>. The ministry had sent the flight time to the deputy commissioners, UNOs and expatriate-welfare offices, which told the families <sup>[N30](#n30)</sup>. Ministry officials handed the bodies to relatives after airport formalities, and they were buried in Naogaon and Natore that afternoon and evening <sup>[N139](#n139)</sup>. Each family was to get Tk 35,000 at once for burial and a Tk 3 lakh grant after three months, the Atrai UNO said <sup>[N139](#n139)</sup>.

<a id="cases"></a>

#### 16.1.5 Cases

- **Early 2023, Abdul Jalil Sheikh (35)** went on a Tk 5 lakh loan and came home in a coffin within a year; the note on it read "Cause of death: natural. Post-mortem: not requested. Compensation: none", and his wife, told by co-workers he died of a stroke, was left with two children and the debt (Guardian, March 2024) <sup>[N358 (2024)](#n358)</sup>.
- **July 2024, Mister Ali (38) of Madarganj, Jamalpur,** a street cleaner in a Saudi city for two and a half years, was killed on 28 July when a car hit him from behind while he worked on the road, his family and local people said; his brother asked for the body to be brought home at government cost <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>.
- **March 2025, Kawsar,** who worked on a gas line in Dammam for a company named Sacco, died in a work accident on 3 March, relatives said, citing his acquaintances; the family heard through a migrant neighbour, and a week later his father didn't know through which agency or when the body would come <sup>[N126](#n126)</sup>. On 10 March the father applied in writing at the Mymensingh welfare centre ([If a worker dies in Saudi Arabia, item 2: Ask Bangladesh to bring the body home, or say you want burial in Saudi Arabia](#if-a-worker-dies-in-saudi-arabia-item-2)) <sup>[N126](#n126)</sup>.
- **May 2025, Saiful Islam (28) of Juri, Moulvibazar,** a poultry-farm labourer near Riyadh who had just been given three months' leave, was killed on 20 May when the goods truck he and a co-worker were riding to Riyadh on their day off hit a camel and overturned <sup>[N365](#n365)</sup>.
- **July 2025, Sakibur Rahman** died a month and a half after going abroad; the Magnama Union Parishad panel chairman said papers were being collected to bring the body home <sup>[N265](#n265)</sup>.
- **October 2025, Habib Khan** died of a heart attack in a Mecca hospital with his Tk 5.5 lakh loan unpaid; his father said worry over lack of work made him ill <sup>[N240](#n240)</sup>.
- **December 2025, Nurul Alam:** his father rushed to send the papers to bring the body home, and no one knew when it would arrive; the Adhunagar Union Parishad chairman said the Union Parishad and upazila administration were helping <sup>[N266](#n266)</sup>.
- **March 2026, Mofizur Rahman (65) of Patiya, Chattogram,** 12 years in Makkah at whatever job he found, was hit by a car walking home from prayers on 9 March and died in hospital; his kafil (sponsor) confirmed the death to the family, and at his dying request to the kafil he was to be buried in Makkah <sup>[N366](#n366)</sup>.
- **March 2026, Abdullah Al Mamun:** the Gaffargaon UNO said efforts were starting through the Welfare Board to bring the body home, and Mymensingh's deputy commissioner said the UNO had been told to plan help for the family <sup>[N355](#n355)</sup>. The body reached Dhaka on 6 April and the expatriates' welfare minister received it <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>; at the funeral the Board gave the family Tk 35,000 and Tk 50,000 in cash, with Tk 3 lakh to follow <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>.
- **March 2026, Azgar Bepari (38) of Dohar, Dhaka,** who went through a broker on borrowed money and had worked illegally for a year as a welder's helper, fell from a roof in Riyadh on 24 March and died; the family had heard bringing the body home would cost Tk 4 lakh, which they couldn't pay <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>. The Dohar UNO's answer is under If a worker dies in Saudi Arabia, item 2 <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>.
- **April 2026, Sobahan Khan (42) of West Maijpara, Dasar, Madaripur,** who had gone on 19 November 2025, fell from the lift of an under-construction building in Riyadh on 7 April, was taken by co-workers to Al-Jazeera hospital with a head injury and died on 11 April; the family, left without its only earner, learned of it the next night and asked for the body to be brought home through the government <sup>[N367](#n367)</sup>. Madaripur's additional deputy commissioner said the district would help bring the body home if instructed through the Foreign Ministry, and help the family if it applied <sup>[N367](#n367)</sup>.
- **April 2026, Murad Sheikh (34) of Goalanda, Rajbari,** who had gone a year earlier through his brother working there, died in a road accident; his brother said the company gave no help, and the body came home on 9 May, 15 days later, paid for by a Goalanda expatriate with the embassy's help <sup>[N368](#n368)</sup>. Prothom Alo later gave the time as 16 days <sup>[N176](#n176)</sup>.
- **May 2026, Ripon Mia (37) of Madan, Netrokona,** at a private company since October 2025, was killed near Madinah when a lorry hit his taxi; the acting UNO of Kendua said the papers had been collected and the ministry contacted <sup>[N356](#n356)</sup>.
- **May 2026, Shakil:** his father said bringing the body home needed about SR 8,000, which the family didn't have; after the local MP, a state minister, intervened, the body reached Dhaka with government help around midnight on 12 June and his home 28 days after the death <sup>[N270](#n270)</sup>.
- **July 2026, Foyez Ahmed and Sujon Ahmed of Noakhali,** brothers carrying dates in the same vehicle, died in a road accident on 16 July; the bodies arrived on 28 July and the Welfare Board paid Tk 35,000 each for burial, their father said <sup>[N245](#n245)</sup>.
- **July 2026,** a migrant from Kanaighat, Sylhet, died in a road accident <sup>[N369](#n369)</sup>.
- **August 2026, Md Kamrul Zaman (37) of Begumganj, Noakhali,** who worked at a shopping mall in Taif, was shot dead at dawn on 30 August while phoning his wife after his shift; his cousin said the gunman was a young Saudi who then shot himself <sup>[N370](#n370)</sup>.
- **August 2026, Md Sohag of Companiganj, Noakhali,** died of a reported heart attack in Abha on 31 August; his sister said his iqama and passport had expired months earlier and he couldn't renew them, and his body lay in a hospital cold store <sup>[N371](#n371)</sup>. Their father had also died there, in 2003, and was buried there because the family couldn't bring the body home, she said <sup>[N371](#n371)</sup>.
- **September 2026, Abdul Karim of Kanaighat, Sylhet,** on his way to Jeddah airport with a ticket home after about three years, and his neighbour Khalid Ahmad were killed when their car crashed at Al-Khurma on 7 September <sup>[N369](#n369)</sup>.
- **September 2026, Mohammad Shafiq (52) of Matlab Uttar, Chandpur,** a labourer near Dammam for about 26 years, was hit by a speeding car while cycling to work on 10 September and declared dead at a local hospital, his family said <sup>[N372](#n372)</sup>. Eleven days later the body was still in the morgue; the family had contacted BMET but not yet reached the embassy, and the Matlab Uttar UNO said the upazila would help with bringing the body home and the burial <sup>[N372](#n372)</sup>.
- **October 2026, Sajib Hossain:** the Brahmanpara UNO said his father and brother, also in Saudi Arabia, would lead the effort to bring the body home <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>.
- **October 2026, Md Shahed Ahammed,** killed at Riyadh airport on 10 October (see [Current situation](#current-situation)), had twice gone to Saudi Arabia before and come home because he could not find work; this time he stayed with his father-in-law, got a company driver's job through him by handing in his driving licence, and later moved to Jeddah <sup>[N373](#n373)</sup>. He had come home on leave on 7 September and gone back a month and three days later <sup>[N373](#n373)</sup>. His body reached Dhaka on the morning of 23 October, where the state minister for foreign affairs received it and handed it to the family, and the government gave the family a cheque for financial assistance <sup>[N373](#n373)</sup>.

<a id="what-the-family-can-claim"></a>

### 16.2 What the family can claim

- **Tk 3 lakh Welfare Board grant,** for workers who left with BMET clearance or joined the Board through the embassy; the Board writes listing the papers <sup>[103 (2024)](#o103), [76](#o76)</sup>. **You'll need** <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>:
  - a family-members certificate from the Union Parishad chairman, mayor or councillor;
  - an undertaking on Tk 400 non-judicial stamp paper (samples on [wewb.gov.bd](https://www.wewb.gov.bd/));
  - a statement of the receiving account at any scheduled bank showing account and routing numbers;
  - 2 passport-size colour photos of each heir.

  Send them to the Board; it is free and pays into the account, with an SMS, within 60 working days of complete papers <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>, though families often have to chase it repeatedly <sup>[N161](#n161)</sup>. In April 2026 the Board also gave one family Tk 50,000 in cash at the funeral, on top of the Tk 35,000, with the Tk 3 lakh to follow <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - When does the Board pay Tk 50,000 in cash at the funeral, and to which families?
- **Tk 10 lakh life insurance,** for workers who left with BMET clearance from 1 January 2023, covering 5 years from the BMET card's issue <sup>[154](#o154)</sup>. No application if the body comes home; if buried in Saudi Arabia or died after returning, apply at [portal.wewb.gov.bd](https://portal.wewb.gov.bd/) or the Board with the BMET card and passport copy; free, paid within 30 working days <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>. Claims recorded as suicide are often rejected. Middlemen offer to speed up payouts for a large cut; the claim is free and needs no middleman <sup>[N228](#n228)</sup>. Amirun Begum of Narsingdi, who went in November 2022 (before this insurance began), died in September 2023 by suicide, by the Saudi death certificate; when her father asked at Probashi Kallyan Bhaban in Dhaka in January 2024 about the Tk 3 lakh, a staff member looked at the certificate and said that because she had killed herself she wouldn't get the insurance money <sup>[N359 (2024)](#n359)</sup>.
- **Compensation and unpaid wages from Saudi Arabia.** With the heirs' power of attorney, the embassy pursues them in Saudi courts and sends the money through BMET <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>. The ambassador said at the embassy's National Expatriates' Day meeting (reported January 2024) that the embassy had contracted two Saudi law firms to recover dead workers' unpaid wages and dues, and that Tk 33 crore owed to the families of 65 dead Bangladeshis had been recovered and sent home the previous December <sup>[N57 (2024)](#n57)</sup>. The Board tells the family when compensation is possible; the family prepares a power of attorney (the Board's legal cell helps) and the same four papers as for the grant; the Board pays heirs within 60 working days of receiving the money, free <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>. A relative can also email the ministry (aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd) with proof of migrant status, the employer's name and full address and what is owed; it instructs the embassy and Board within 7 working days, free <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>.
- **GOSI work-death compensation.** For a work-injury death, GOSI's work-injury insurance (who it covers: Injured or ill) pays the family 84 times the average monthly wage registered with it, plus repatriation <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. Claim at a GOSI office (toll-free 199044), or from Bangladesh through the embassy with a power of attorney, as for compensation and unpaid wages above <sup>[156](#o156), [61](#o61), [76](#o76)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Which papers does GOSI need from the family for a work-death claim?
  > - How long does GOSI take to pay?

<a id="injured-or-ill"></a>

### 16.3 Injured or ill

**To get home:** if you are injured or seriously ill in Saudi Arabia, you or your family apply to the Welfare Board with BMET clearance or membership and a passport copy; free, the Board gets the embassy to bring the worker home, sometimes into hospital, and the airport desk takes them to hospital by ambulance <sup>[76](#o76), [1](#o1)</sup>.

**To get home without valid papers:** the Board can't help directly; it forwards the family's application to the ministry, which writes to the embassy's labour wing <sup>[N374](#n374)</sup>. An employer's case against the worker can hold up the return, as in Mizanur Rahman's case below <sup>[N374](#n374)</sup>.

**Treatment in Saudi Arabia:** the employer must insure you, but the cover doesn't pay for serious illness (see Housing, food and health care) <sup>[9](#o9), [23](#o23)</sup>. A company worker gets up to 120 days' sick leave a year, 30 at full pay, 60 at three-quarters, 30 unpaid <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>; a domestic worker up to 30 days with a medical report, 15 on full pay, the rest on half <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>.

**Treatment of a work injury:** the rules in this paragraph come from the Social Insurance Law of 2024 (Royal Decree M/273 of 2 July 2024), in force from the day after it was issued <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. That law does not apply to a worker who already had GOSI contribution periods from before it came into force and has not been paid compensation for them; the earlier Social Insurance Law (Royal Decree M/33 of 3/9/1421 Hijri, 2000) still applies to such a worker <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. Under the 2024 law, GOSI covers a work injury only if it happened after your GOSI registration was complete, or within the time the rules give the employer to register you (or you, if the employer refuses or delays) <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. If covered, GOSI gives medical care for as long as your condition needs it, at the hospitals and clinics it specifies <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. If the injury was reported in the way and within the time the rules set, then for each day you can't work, holidays included, from the day after the injury, GOSI pays a daily allowance of 100% of your daily registered wage in the month before the month of the injury, 75% while it pays for your treatment in any hospital, medical centre or the like <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. The allowance stops when you can work again, recover or die, or when permanent disability is established <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What are the time limits and method for registering a worker and for reporting a work injury to GOSI?
> - What medical care, daily allowance and lump sums does the earlier Social Insurance Law (2000) give a worker who was registered with GOSI before July 2024?

**GOSI work-injury compensation:** GOSI insures workers against work injury at the employer's cost; domestic workers only if its board decides so <sup>[90](#o90), [155](#o155)</sup>. If a work injury leaves 50-99% or total permanent disability, GOSI pays a non-Saudi worker a lump sum; claim at a GOSI office (toll-free 199044) <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>. Under the 2024 law (for who it covers, see Treatment of a work injury), the monthly benefit behind the lump sum is 100% of your average registered wage over the three months before the month of the injury for total disability, or that times your disability percentage for 50% or more <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. The lump sum is 84 times that monthly benefit for total disability and 60 times for partial disability <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. Below 50% disability you get a lump sum of 60 times the partial-disability monthly benefit if you were 45 or under on the day of the injury <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. If you were older, take off one month's benefit for each year over 45, counting part of a year of six months or more as a full year <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. Whatever the age, the under-50% lump sum is at least 36 times the assumed monthly benefit <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are domestic workers now covered by GOSI work-injury insurance?
> - Which papers does GOSI need, and how long does it take to pay?
> - Can a worker who has already come home claim from Bangladesh through the embassy?

**Treatment money after coming home:** the Board pays up to Tk 2 lakh <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>; a Board assistant director said in June 2025 that it could give a registered worker up to Tk 1.5 lakh quickly <sup>[N375](#n375)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the Board's treatment grant after coming home up to Tk 2 lakh or up to Tk 1.5 lakh?

**You'll need** <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>:

- BMET clearance card or membership;
- passport copy;
- medical papers;
- a 6R colour photo.

Apply on the Board's portal, at a district welfare centre or at the Board; it checks by video call and pays within 60 working days <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

**Disability insurance:** permanent disability within the 5 years pays 10% to 100% of Tk 10 lakh <sup>[21](#o21), [76](#o76)</sup>. **You'll need** <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>:

- BMET clearance card;
- passport copy;
- medical papers;
- a 6R photo showing the disability.

Apply free at the Board, paid within 30 working days (Deputy Director Welfare-2, +88 02 48317085, 01321-158354, dd.welfare2@wewb.gov.bd) <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

**What illness costs.** An OKUP (Ovibashi Karmi Unnayan Program, a migrant workers' organisation) study in December 2024 found returnees came back with back pain, workplace injuries, heart disease, kidney problems, cancer, high blood pressure, tumours and skin disease; some borrowed from home for treatment, and many came home because they couldn't get treatment <sup>[N115 (2024)](#n115)</sup>.

**Cases:**

- **2021, Abul Hashem (42) of Lohagara, Chattogram,** in Saudi Arabia since 2005 with two shops in Mecca, had to wind up the business and come home after a stomach cancer diagnosis, and spent about Tk 68 lakh on treatment; by January 2026 he farmed two acres with technical advice, incentives and training from the upazila agriculture office <sup>[N376](#n376)</sup>.
- **May 2024, Jabbar,** who worked without papers at a tile factory owned by an Indian national, was badly injured in a road accident on 10 May 2024 and put in a Saudi hospital ICU <sup>[N164](#n164)</sup>. His wife said people she spoke to three months later asked Tk 70 lakh to send him home because he had no papers, and later others she couldn't identify asked Tk 70 lakh and then Tk 19,000 for the airfare, which she couldn't raise <sup>[N164](#n164)</sup>. She applied in writing to the Board's director general on 13 January 2025; the Board wrote to the embassy and sent a reminder on 9 April, the Board's assistant director said <sup>[N375](#n375)</sup>. She said her application brought no result until the Tanore UNO (upazila nirbahi officer, the upazila's chief government officer), whose office she waited 22 days to enter, reached the embassy after 15 days of trying <sup>[N164](#n164)</sup>. He was flown home unconscious on 23 May 2025, after more than a year in the ICU <sup>[N375](#n375)</sup>. The escorts who had promised to take him to a hospital with a doctor and nurse, and to give some money, gave nothing and left him at the airport; a private hospital in Dhaka cost Tk 70,000 for seven hours before his wife hired an oxygen-equipped vehicle to Rajshahi for Tk 25,000, she said <sup>[N164](#n164)</sup>.
- **February 2025, Mizanur Rahman (62) of Noakhali,** a day labourer in Jubail since 1993 and without valid papers since 2017, his family said, was admitted to Jubail General Hospital after a stroke on 28 February; his first week's bill was waived but treatment largely stopped afterwards because no one guaranteed the bill, the family said <sup>[N374](#n374)</sup>. His wife said the embassy told the family it would send him home once a compensation case his employer had filed against him was resolved; the employer had since died but the case remained, and she didn't know how to resolve it from Bangladesh <sup>[N374](#n374)</sup>. Her application of 9 April 2025, made with help from BRAC's Migration Programme, went from the Board to the ministry, which wrote to the labour wing on 16 April asking it to send him home urgently <sup>[N374](#n374)</sup>.

<a id="job-lost-within-6-months"></a>

### 16.4 Job lost within 6 months

If you lose the job within 6 months and come home, the migrant worker insurance pays Tk 50,000 <sup>[21](#o21), [76](#o76)</sup>. **You'll need** <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>:

- BMET clearance card;
- passport copy;
- copies of the departure and arrival stamp pages.

Apply free at the Board, paid within 30 working days (01712-107301, jahanwewb@gmail.com) <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>. Illness from before leaving isn't covered, and those returning for serious illness are often refused <sup>[N228](#n228)</sup>.

Shariful Islam of Tangail went in October 2023 at a cost of Tk 5 lakh, worked 22 days, was detained by police and sent home on an outpass in December; in January 2024 he said he didn't know why he was sent back or whether he had paid for insurance, and that he had applied nowhere and nobody had told him to <sup>[N359 (2024)](#n359)</sup>.

<a id="if-the-board-doesnt-deliver"></a>

### 16.5 If the Board doesn't deliver

Complain in this order <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>:

1. The officer named for the service.
2. The Board's grievance officer (+88 02 55138267, 01684-501891, azizwelfare@gmail.com, 30 working days).
3. The ministry's Joint Secretary (Administration) (+88 0241030261, jsadmin@probashi.gov.bd, 20 working days).
4. [grs.gov.bd](https://www.grs.gov.bd/), the government complaint system (60 working days).

<a id="mental-health"></a>

### 16.6 Mental health

The Saudi Health Ministry's psychological consultation line, 920033360, takes calls from anyone in Saudi Arabia, 8 am to 8 pm (Saturday from 1 pm) <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>. After coming home, BRAC's Migration Programme gives counselling ([At Dhaka airport](#at-dhaka-airport)) <sup>[N354](#n354)</sup>.

In an OKUP (Ovibashi Karmi Unnayan Program, a migrant workers' organisation) survey presented in December 2024 of 1,096 returnees from six Middle East countries who came for health services, 55% had come back from Saudi Arabia and 48% came with mental health problems, more often men; domestic workers reported mental problems most <sup>[N115 (2024)](#n115)</sup>. Khokon Howlader of Mirzaganj, Patuakhali, seven or eight years in Saudi Arabia, hanged himself on 24 September 2024 while on a video call with his wife and twin sons, the family said; his body reached Dhaka 22 days later <sup>[N377 (2024)](#n377)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does 920033360 answer in Bangla or English?
> - Does the embassy labour wing give counselling?
> - Does the Probashi call centre (16135) give counselling?

<a id="coming-home"></a>

## 17 Coming home

<a id="what-you-can-bring-home"></a>

### 17.1 What you can bring home

- Duty-free yearly: 2 used phones and 1 new; 100 g gold and 200 g silver jewellery, at most 12 pieces of one kind; 200 cigarettes; and household items such as a TV up to 29 inches <sup>[158](#o158)</sup>.
- Gold: up to 117 g (10 tola), or one 10-tola bar, once a year if declared, at Tk 5,000 duty per tola; more is confiscated and undeclared bars mean arrest and a criminal case <sup>[158](#o158)</sup>.
- No alcohol on a Bangladeshi passport; goods to sell are seized <sup>[158](#o158)</sup>.
- Cash over USD 10,000 or equivalent: declare at customs on the FMJ form (the foreign-currency declaration form) <sup>[158](#o158)</sup>.

Declare goods on the form given on the plane, or at the custom house beside the airport within 7 days <sup>[158](#o158)</sup>. Customs: 13600 <sup>[158](#o158)</sup>.

<a id="at-dhaka-airport"></a>

### 17.2 At Dhaka airport

- The Probashi Kallyan (expatriate welfare) desk helps with immigration, customs and the airline, free, and takes complaints from returnees abused or cheated abroad (+88 02 8901040, 01321-158352) <sup>[76](#o76), [N252](#n252)</sup>.
- Women returning within a year after abuse, torture or fraud get Tk 5,000 there. Bring <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>:
  - passport;
  - BMET clearance;
  - visa;
  - ticket;
  - air waybill.
- BRAC's Migration Programme helps returnees at the airport and with reintegration, counselling and jobs <sup>[N354](#n354)</sup>: Information Center, Rahman Villa (2nd floor), 402 Ashkona, Dakshinkhan, Dhaka, opposite the airport; 01700797182, 01700797183 <sup>[N378](#n378)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Does BRAC's Information Center serve workers returning from Saudi Arabia?

<a id="a-place-to-stay"></a>

### 17.3 A place to stay

- The Welfare Board's Wage Earners' Centre near Shahjalal airport: book at [wec.wewb.gov.bd](http://wec.wewb.gov.bd/wewb-centre/booking/search) or in person with BMET clearance or Board membership, passport copy and ticket; Tk 100 to register, Tk 200 a night, free airport transport (01321-158301, bwec@wewb.gov.bd) <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.
- The airport's Probashi Lounge gives workers with BMET clearance food at up to 30% off and free wifi, phone and printing <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

<a id="legal-help-at-home"></a>

### 17.4 Legal help at home

For legal help inside Bangladesh, for example if your family is threatened, apply to your district probashi kallyan centre or the Board, which asks the local administration to act, free <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>. The centres are listed with contacts under "District Probashi Kallyan Centre list" on [wewb.gov.bd](https://wewb.gov.bd/) <sup>[159](#o159)</sup>.

**Kidnapping for ransom.** On 11 January 2025 a gang of expatriate Bangladeshis kidnapped Russell, a Bangladeshi long resident in Saudi Arabia, from Riyadh, tortured him and got his family to pay Tk 35 lakh ransom into a bank account the gang gave, according to Khilgaon police in Dhaka <sup>[N379](#n379)</sup>. After his father-in-law filed a case at Khilgaon police station on 21 January, police arrested three men in Lakshmipur and Brahmanbaria on 31 January, and Tk 12 lakh of the ransom was frozen by court order <sup>[N379](#n379)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which papers must a returnee bring to the district centre or the Board for legal help inside Bangladesh?
> - How long does the local administration take to act?

<a id="reintegration-loan-pkb"></a>

### 17.5 Reintegration loan (PKB)

The Probashi Kallyan Bank (PKB) reintegration loan for starting a business lends up to Tk 50 lakh over up to 10 years at 9% simple interest <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>.

Security depends on the amount <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>:

- up to Tk 3 lakh: no collateral;
- Tk 3-5 lakh: a co-guarantee;
- above that: a registered land mortgage.

1. <a id="reintegration-loan-pkb-item-1"></a>**You'll need** <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>:
   - the free application form;
   - 3 passport-size photos;
   - passport and NID copies;
   - a present and permanent address certificate from the Union Parishad or municipality;
   - a guarantor (relative or well-off person) with 2 photos, NID or passport copy, address certificate and a clean CIB report (the Credit Information Bureau report, Bangladesh Bank's record of a person's loans);
   - a current trade licence (compulsory);
   - a business description with address and 2 years' income and spending;
   - proof of rent or ownership of the premises;
   - a declaration of your own investment and other loans;
   - your papers from abroad;
   - any training certificate;
   - 3 signed cheque leaves each from the business's and the guarantor's accounts, with statements.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How does a guarantor get a CIB report?

2. <a id="reintegration-loan-pkb-item-2"></a>**Apply at the PKB branch where the business is** <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>; fees are 0.5% processing and 0.5% documentation, each at most Tk 15,000, and the decision comes within 15 working days of a complete application (16238, or +880 9677-787878 from abroad) <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>.
3. <a id="reintegration-loan-pkb-item-3"></a>**If the branch doesn't resolve a problem,** complain in this order:
   1. PKB's grievance officer, the General Manager (Administration and Accounts) (gm2@pkb.gov.bd, +88-02-48321195, 30 working days) <sup>[40](#o40)</sup>.
   2. Its Deputy Managing Director (dmd@pkb.gov.bd, +88-02-48319468, 20 working days) <sup>[40](#o40)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Can an applicant refused a PKB reintegration loan appeal the decision?
   > - Can a refused applicant reapply?

It began in April 2019 as a pilot under an agreement between the ministry and the ILO (International Labour Organization), is now funded by the Wage Earners' Welfare Board, and had taken more than 2 lakh calls by October 2025; a worker in Bisha said it took several tries to get through and operators couldn't check his e-passport status, telling him to check online himself <sup>[N81](#n81)</sup>.
- **Bangladesh Hajj Office, Jeddah** (under Bangladesh's Ministry of Religious Affairs): missionhajj@gmail.com <sup>[N381](#n381)</sup>. It advertises some posts to Bangladeshis in Saudi Arabia with a valid iqama and passport: in October 2026, protocol assistant and computer operator cum interpreter at SR 4,425-6,875, caretaker cum night guard at SR 2,815-5,090 and drivers at SR 3,250-5,525 a month, age limit 32 <sup>[N381](#n381)</sup>. Applications go by hand or by email, with a handwritten application and CV, 2 passport photos, iqama and passport copies and attested copies of certificates <sup>[N381](#n381)</sup>.
- **Probashi call centre (Welfare Board):** 16135 free from Bangladesh, 09610102030, +8808000102030 from abroad, pbcc@wewb.gov.bd <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.
- **Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare:**

  - Escalation: Mission and Welfare wing head, adsmission@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030219 <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>.
- **Airport Probashi Kallyan desk, Dhaka:** +88 02 8901040, 01321-158352, welfaredesk@gmail.com <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.
- **BRAC Information Center, Dhaka airport:** 01700797182, 01700797183 <sup>[N378](#n378)</sup>.
- **Probashi Kallyan Bank:** 16238, +880 9677-787878 from abroad <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>.
- **Bangladesh Bank complaints (banks, bKash):** 16236, bb.cipc@bb.org.bd <sup>[150](#o150)</sup>.
- **Hajj and Umrah helpline (Bangladesh):** 16136, +880 9602666707 from abroad, office hours <sup>[133](#o133)</sup>.
- **Saudi labour ministry (MHRSD):** 19911, Sunday to Thursday, 8 am to 5 pm, Arabic and English; for Bangla, the embassy labour wing's 8001000125 <sup>[102 (2024)](#o102), [78](#o78), [79](#o79)</sup>. Online complaints via its e-services, answered within 5 working days; report labour-law breaches through its Labor Law Violation e-service <sup>[78](#o78), [32 (2024)](#o32)</sup>.
  - Qiwa: 920000105 <sup>[147](#o147)</sup>.
